CORE FEATURES

A safe and peaceful environment
Relevant and responsive to the changing needs of our
students and the society
Academic autonomy and flexibility
Research Ambiance
Nature of faculty and students:
— Faculty recruiting norms are much higher than
most of the academic institutes in India
— Students are inducted strictly on a merit basis
Sustainable and all-inclusive growth, including
community outreach programmes
Infrastructure: Liberal funding to the laboratory facilities
and amenities to make them comparable to those best
in the world
Administration: Exclusive concern of IIT Gandhinagar,
and handled internally
— Director given adequate powers to manage most
academic, administrative and financial issues
(within the framework)
Residential Campus:
— Leads to closer academic and social interaction
between students and faculty
— Develops stronger community spirit and provides
opportunity to learn from each other
— Sustained academic ambiance resulting in higher
creativity from everyone.

Dedication to the Institute

MISSION

IIT Gandhinagar, as an institution for higher learning in science,
technology and related fields, aspires to develop top-notch
scientists, engineers, leaders and entrepreneurs to meet the
needs of the society-now and in the future. Furthermore, in this
land of Gandhiji, with his spirit of high work ethic and service to
the society, IIT Gandhinagar seeks to undertake ground breaking
research, and develop breakthrough products that will improve
everyday lives of our communities.

»

Co-operation, Collaboration and Team Work

To build and develop a world-class institution for creating
and imparting knowledge at the undergraduate, post
graduate and doctoral levels, contributing to the
development of the nation and the humanity at large.
To develop leaders with vision, creative thinking, social
awareness and respect for our values.
To foster excellence in teaching and research to make a
global impact.
To engage in path-breaking research that would influence
national policies.
To pursue sustainable technological solutions to societal
problems.
To focus on lean engineering solutions for sustainable
development.
To be the leader for academic and industrial collaborations
in various disciplines, nationally and internationally.
To create awareness of the true significance of learning
and teaching.
To enrich local schools and communities through valueadded interactions.
To encourage excellent language skills as part of the
institutional culture.
To prepare students not just for their first job, but their last
job as well.

»
»

»

»
»

To shape IIT Gandhinagar into an exciting place for learning,
teaching and research.
To establish a process of learning that is free, fulfilling and
enjoyable experience.
To provide an enabling environment to nurture critical and
creative minds, and to propel them to greater heights of
excellence in their pursuits.
To create a vibrant atmosphere that breeds front runner
innovators, scientists, engineers, entrepreneurs,
academicians and thinkers of tomorrow.
To provide opportunity for students to learn from wherever,
however and whatever they choose to study.
To make IIT Gandhinagar the preferred destination for
future generations of students, staff and faculty.

Annual Report 2025-26 | www.iitgn.ac.in

FROM THE
DIRECTOR’S DESK
The year 2025–26 has been one of purposeful growth, innovation, and
meaningful engagement for IIT Gandhinagar. As an institute committed to
academic excellence and societal impact, we continued to strengthen our
foundations while expanding our work across research, infrastructure,
sustainability, student engagement, and global partnerships.
Our commitment to sustainable campus development received significant
national recognition during the year. The Student Hostel Project was honoured
with the Architecture Excellence Recognition 2025 in the Educational
Institution Design category by the PHD Chamber of Commerce and Industry.
The Guest House, Hostels, and Director’s Residence received a 4-Star GRIHA
Provisional Rating, with the plaque presented at the GRIHA Summit in
November 2025. IITGN was ranked First (Joint Winner) at the 6th National
Water Awards 2024 by the Ministry of Jal Shakti in the Best Institution
category (Other than School & College Inside-Campus), recognising our
leadership in water conservation and management. The Institute also received
the Bhumi Campus Award 2025 – Most Eco-Friendly Campus, reaffirming
our sustained commitment to environmental stewardship. In addition, IIT
Gandhinagar was awarded Gold in the Industry–Academia Partnership
category at the CII Global Summit on Industry–Academia Partnership
2025, recognising impactful collaborations with industry. The Institute was
also featured prominently in the India Today–MDRA Survey 2025, securing
the 8th position among India’s top engineering colleges and ranking 1st
among emerging government engineering institutions.
Infrastructure expansion remained a key priority. Construction of the Phase
2A student hostels (capacity: approximately 1,200 students) and 182 faculty
and staff housing units progressed steadily during the year. Work continued
on the Fire Engineering Research Laboratory (FERL), while the Transgenic
Greenhouse facility was completed. The Institute also initiated the
development of a state-of-the-art cleanroom facility and strengthened
campus power systems with the installation of a 1000 kVA dry-type transformer
at the Research Park, along with upgrades to the main receiving substation,
ensuring reliable and future-ready infrastructure.
IITGN further strengthened its academic and research collaborations through
a range of strategic national and international partnerships. A landmark MoU
with the Indian Institute of Science, Bengaluru, enables deeper research
collaboration, faculty exchange, and a direct BTech-to-MTech pathway for
meritorious students. The Institute also expanded global engagement through
partnerships with the Nagoya Institute of Technology, Japan, the VNU
University of Engineering and Technology, Vietnam, and the National Yang
Ming Chiao Tung University, Taiwan. In addition, IITGN strengthened India–Taiwan academic engagement through
collaboration with the Taipei Economic and Cultural Centre, establishing the Taiwan Education Centre on campus

IIT Gandhinagar

5

to promote academic exchange, language learning, and
cooperation in emerging areas such as semiconductor
education and research.

Technology (AIT), Thailand, under a Double Degree
Programme, expanding global academic pathways for
students.

During the year, IITGN expanded its academic and
research footprint through several strategic initiatives.
The launch of the AI Resilience and Command (ARC)
Centre marks a significant step toward data-driven
climate risk management and urban resilience. The
Centre for Creative Learning (CCL) continued to advance
STEM outreach nationally. Recognising the transformative
potential of artificial intelligence, IITGN introduced new
AI-focused programmes, including an MTech in AI and
short-term residential courses. A notable milestone was
the development of the Ganga-1B AI language model,
designed for Hindi, reflecting our commitment to
inclusive and interdisciplinary AI research. Looking
ahead, IITGN plans to introduce Postgraduate and
Doctoral programmes in Maritime Engineering and
Master of Design (MDes) in Integrated Design &
Technology from 2026 onwards, further expanding our
academic offerings and research capabilities in emerging
and industry-relevant fields.

Our students and scholars have brought distinction to
IIT Gandhinagar through national and international
achievements. Himanshu Beniwal received the
Fulbright-Nehru Doctoral Research Fellowship 2025–
26 and the Microsoft Research India PhD Fellowship
2025, while Gayatri Priyadarsini was awarded the
Google PhD Fellowship 2025. Dharaben Rameshbhai
Thakkar earned the ACM India Doctoral Dissertation
Award 2025. In student excellence, our Chess Team
won Gold at the AU Cup 2026, and Pathan Mohammad
Rashid, IITGN’s first para-athlete, clinched Gold in
Wheelchair Table Tennis at the Sangam Para Sports
Event, IIT Delhi, reflecting the Institute’s commitment to
talent, innovation, and inclusive growth.

In a unique milestone for campus engagement, IIT
Gandhinagar became home to Gujarat’s first Gen-Z
themed revamped Post Office, launched as part of India
Post’s nationwide modernisation initiative to transform
post offices into student-centric, technology-enabled
spaces. Inaugurated on December 5, 2025, the initiative
places IITGN among 46 educational campuses across
India selected for this transformation.
The Institute also commenced work on Project
SAMARTH (Silicon and Advanced Semiconductor
Manufacturing Research and Training Hub),
announced during the Gujarat Semiconnect Conference.
The initiative, led by IIT Gandhinagar in collaboration
with the Department of Science and Technology,
Government of Gujarat; the Ministry of Electronics and
Information Technology; and industry partners including
Tata Electronics, aims to build a skilled workforce and
strengthen research and innovation in India’s
semiconductor ecosystem. The Institute also participated
in the ‘Seva Sankalp’ national pledge, aligning with the
Government of India’s vision of Viksit Bharat 2047.
Our faculty and researchers continued to earn national
and international recognition for their contributions to
research and innovation. Prof Nipun Batra received the
ACM SIGEnergy Rising Star Award 2025, while Prof Vimal
Mishra was elected Fellow of the Indian National Science
Academy in recognition of his outstanding contributions
to climate and water research. International engagement
remains integral to our growth. IITGN further
strengthened its collaboration with the Asian Institute of

Community engagement continues to be central to our
mission. Through the NEEV initiative, the Institute
trained over 200 women and youth from neighbouring
rural communities between April and June 2025 in
collaboration with partners such as the Desai Foundation
Trust. Alumni engagement also flourished through
Homecoming 2025 and the Young Alumni Excellence
Awards 2024.
Student engagement and well-being also remained
central to the Institute’s ethos. Programmes such as
NYASA, IIT Gandhinagar’s mental health and wellness
initiative, continued to support students through
counselling, awareness programmes, and well-being
activities. The Institute also nurtured young research
talent through its Summer Research Internship
Programme (SRIP), which attracts outstanding
undergraduate students from across India and abroad
to work with IITGN faculty on cutting-edge research
projects.
The 14th Convocation marked a significant milestone
as IITGN celebrated its largest graduating cohort to date,
conferring degrees across BTech, MTech, MSc, MA, and
PhD programmes, along with Institute Gold Medals and
recognitions for academic excellence and leadership.
The accomplishments of 2025–26 reflect the collective
dedication of our students, faculty, staff, alumni, and
partners. As we look ahead, IIT Gandhinagar remains
firmly committed to advancing knowledge, fostering
innovation, and contributing meaningfully to society.
With renewed purpose and a shared vision, we continue
our journey toward excellence and impact.
Prof Rajat Moona
Director, IIT Gandhinagar

EXECUTIVE MASTERS

In 2025–26, IIT Gandhinagar expanded its executive education portfolio by offering specialized Masters
programs for working professionals. These programs provide rigorous academic training, practical
exposure, and interdisciplinary knowledge to support career growth and organizational impact.

14TH CONVOCATION

IITGN celebrated its 14th Convocation Ceremony on June 21, 2025 at the Mahatma Mandir Convention &
Exhibition Centre in Gandhinagar. The ceremony commenced in the presence of Mr Aditya Ghosh, Cofounder of Akasa Air, who served as the Chief Guest. The Institute conferred degrees to 668 graduating
students, recognising their academic excellence as well as their all-round achievements. Prior to the
convocation, an Award Ceremony was held on June 20, 2025 in the presence of Shri SP Shukla, Chairman,
Mahindra Agri Solutions Ltd, Mahindra EPC Irrigation Ltd & CIE Automotive India Ltd. This year, 56 students
were awarded various medals, including 40 gold medals and 17 silver medals for excellence in various
categories such as academics, outstanding research, innovation, leadership, social service, sports, arts and
culture, among others.

SHORT COURSES

IIT Gandhinagar has been offering short courses since 2010. These are 1-credit courses, typically involving
10 to 12 hours of engagement. The Institute offers an average of 10–11 courses per semester, with a total
of 26 courses conducted during the academic year April 2025 – March 2026.
The courses span diverse topics, from ‘Introduction to Scanning Electron Microscopy (SEM)’ by Mr Vivek
Kumar Singh to ‘Interbasin Water Transfer’ by Prof Sharad K Jain, benefiting students across disciplines.
Short courses enable the following:
a. Promote engagement with global professionals and researchers, diversifying the Institute’s portfolio
of offerings
b. Cover a wide range of topics not typically accommodated within the core curriculum
c. Foster interdisciplinary thinking through open-to-all offerings
List of Short Courses Conducted (April 2025 – March 2026)

Kumar Singh, PMRF Fellow & PhD Scholar, Materials
Engineering, IITGN, April 10-18, 2025

Sciences: A Beginner’s Course – Dr Bhavna Ahlawat,

STUDENT
AFFAIRS
PLACEMENTS, INTERNSHIPS AND HIGHER STUDIES

Out of the 668 students who graduated in July 2025, 42 students opted to pursue higher studies. Among
them, 18 students chose to continue their education at international institutions, while 24 enrolled in
leading institutions within India, reflecting a strong inclination towards advanced academic and research
pursuits.

CAMPUS PLACEMENTS 2025

This year, the Career Development Services (CDS) approached nearly ~4500 organisations. This resulted in
better statistics from all perspectives, including higher placement numbers than in the past years. In total,
248 job offers were received. The UG students secured 84.48% placement of registered students compared
to last year’s value of 82.39%.
The industrial sectors providing opportunities to our students have remained largely unchanged from the
previous year, with a significant focus on core areas. Approximately 80% of job offers continue to originate
from these core sectors. The following organisations offered campus placements to the outgoing batch in
2025.
Name of the Organisations:
Aakash Educational Services Ltd

Atlas Copco (India) Private Ltd.

Unit of GCMMF Ltd, Anand)

SUMMER INTERNSHIP 2025

In the summer of 2025, our students participated in academic and industrial internships hosted by 15
international organisations. Of these, 34 students completed academic internships, while 01 completed
industrial internships in various industries.
On the domestic front, we observed an increase in the number of students availing internship opportunities.
Out of 291 students, 114 are UG third-year, 118 are UG second-year, 17 are UG first-year, and 42 are PG
students who have interned at various organisations. A total of 115 organisations have hosted our UG/PG
students. Additionally, 16 students have undergone semester-long internships at 11 different organisations.

are anticipated to pursue education abroad, while 24 plan to enroll in domestic institutions.

SCHOLARSHIPS
IITGN believes that financial constraints should not become obstacles in the academic pursuit of any
student. The Institute’s extremely liberal financial aid and scholarship programs ensure that no student
feels disadvantaged due to their financial situation. The Institute has thus constituted numerous scholarships
and financial assistance mechanisms such as Donor Scholarships, Excellence Scholarships, TML-FAP (Tata
Motors Ltd. Financial Aid Programme), tuition fee waivers, etc. In addition to the above, the institute also
provides financial support in the form of financial grants, and interest-free short/long-term loans to
deserving students for catering to their needs towards expenditures such as tuition fees, hostel and mess
fees, books, computer, pocket expenses, medical emergencies (beyond what is covered by insurance),
social and cultural activities, internships and educational tours, etc.

FINANCIAL AID TO STUDENTS
FINANCIAL AID FOR UNDERGRADUATE STUDENTS

As per the Ministry norms, students with parental income of less than Rs 1 lakh get a full tuition fee
waiver, while students with parental income between Rs 1 lakh and Rs 5 lakh get a waiver of 2/3rd of
the fee. IITGN provides an additional 1/3rd tuition fee reimbursement to students with parental
income ranging between Rs 1 lakh to Rs 2.5 lakhs. Therefore, they effectively receive a full tuition fee
exemption for their education at IITGN. This assistance is provided from the Student Benevolent &
Welfare Fund or the Excellence Funds under the Endowment account.
A total of 53 BTech students received this additional tuition fee waiver during the academic year 202526.

FINANCIAL AID FOR MASTERS STUDENTS

Students with parental income up to Rs 2.5 lakhs are provided with a full tuition fee reimbursement
for their education at IITGN. This assistance is provided from the Student Benevolent & Welfare Fund
or the Excellence Funds in the Endowment.
A total of 108 Masters (M.Tech., M.Sc., and M.A.) students received full tuition fee waivers during the
academic year 2025-26.

FREE BASIC MESSING AND POCKET ALLOWANCE ASSISTANCE

All students of the SC/ST category avail of a full tuition fee waiver. In addition, the Institute provides
free basic messing and a nominal pocket allowance of Rs. 250/- pm for 10 months to SC/ST students
whose annual parental income is up to Rs. 4.5 Lakhs. This assistance is provided to SC/ST Students of
B.Tech., M.Sc., and M.A.
A total of 61 undergraduate and 33 postgraduate SC/ST category students whose annual parental
income was within the limit prescribed for this assistance were granted the facility for free basic
messing and a pocket allowance of ₹ 250 per month for ten months during the academic year 202526.

SCHOLARSHIP FOR STUDENTS
PROF M H DIVEKAR SCHOLARSHIP

The Prof M H Divekar Scholarship was instituted by Mrs Mugdha Mukund Divekar in the year 2014 and
is open for third-year BTech students of Chemical Engineering. The scholarship amount is ₹40,000/and is awarded every year to the student securing the highest grade in the Chemical Engineering
course at the end of the third year.
Tripti Vishwakarma and Ishan Agarwal are the recipients of this scholarship for the academic year
2025-26.

SATYARAM SCHOLARSHIP

The Satyaram Scholarships were instituted by the former Indian Cricketer and philanthropist V V S
Laxman in the year 2016. Students with a minimum CPI of 6.5 and whose family annual income is not
more than Rs. 3 lakhs are eligible for this scholarship. The scholarship amount is ₹ 1 lakh per year per
student and a total of 2 students were awarded in the year 2024-25. The recipient student continues
to get the scholarship support till the completion of his/her BTech Program at IITGN, subject to
meeting the eligibility criteria. The awardee is expected to financially help at least one needy IITGN
student in the future. Aakash Kushwah and Bothkurwar Sai Krishna are the recipients of this
scholarship for the academic year 2025-26.

Annual Report 2025-26 | www.iitgn.ac.in

IIT Gandhinagar

61

RESEARCH ACTIVITIES:
EVENTS AND OUTREACH
Conferences, workshops, symposia, and seminars are integral to
academic engagement, encouraging meaningful discussions on
diverse and important subjects. These collaborative activities often
involve participation from external organisations and help
strengthen the institute’s visibility at various levels. The following
events were conducted during 2025–26:

CONFERENCES

Faculty members from various departments
participated in national and international
conferences and workshops during the year. These
included events such as Conference on Traditions of
Science: Narmada and Water Management, Changing
Landscape of Science & Technology Libraries (CLSTL
2025), CRSI-NSC-2025 at IITGN, The Indian Academic
Makerspaces Summit, Curiosity Conference, and
Women-FORCE. Faculty also contributed to
specialized events like Frontiers of Engineering in
Healthcare Applications, ORBIT, and a Workshop on
3D
Printed
Concrete
Structures,
reflecting
interdisciplinary engagement and collaboration
with external institutions.

CRSI NATIONAL CHEMISTRY SYMPOSIUM

The 35th CRSI National Symposium in Chemistry
(CRSI NSC) and CRSI-ACS Lecture Series was held at
IIT Gandhinagar from July 3–5, 2025. The three-day
event brought together scientists, researchers, and
experts from across India to discuss recent
advances in chemical sciences and promote
collaboration. The symposium featured keynote
and invited lectures by leading scientists from
institutions such as IISc, IITs, IISERs, BARC, DRDO,
and others. A Memorandum of Understanding
(MoU) was also signed between the Chemical
Research Society of India (CRSI) and the Società
Chimica Italiana (SCI), strengthening international

collaboration. The programme included technical
sessions, poster presentations, and demonstrations,
along with the presentation of awards recognising
outstanding contributions in chemistry.

RISE CONCLAVE

The Springer Nature RISE Summit 2025 at IITGN
focused on Research Integrity in Science and
Education (RISE), an initiative promoting ethical
research
practices,
transparency,
and
accountability. The summit included insightful talks
by Mr Steven Inchcoombe, President - Research,
Springer Nature (SN); Prof Rajat Moona, Director,
IITGN; Prof Dr Rajul K Gajjar, Vice Chancellor,
Gujarat Technological University; Prof Shailendra
Saraf, Director, NIPER Ahmedabad, along with
several professionals from the SN India team.
Topics covered included indexing platforms and
evaluation metrics, peer-review pipelines, openaccess publications, predatory journal issues, and
industry collaboration, among others.

62

Annual Report 2025-26 | www.iitgn.ac.in

GANIT SYMPOSIUM

IITGN successfully hosted the three-day GANIT
Symposium on ‘Connections among Algebraic
Geometry, Commutative Algebra and Number
Theory’ from Jan 17–19, 2026. The symposium
featured mini-courses on advanced topics delivered
by Marc Chardin (Institut de Mathématiques de
Jussieu – Université Pierre et Marie Curie), Hussein
Mourtada (Institut de Mathématiques de Jussieu–
Paris), Hoang Le Truong (Vietnam National
University, Hanoi), Arnab Saha, and Prof Jugal K.
Verma (IIT Gandhinagar). In addition, 15 posters
and short talks by graduate students and young
researchers fostered active academic exchange
and discussion. The event was coordinated by Prof
Indranath Sengupta.

ANRF CEO VISITS IITGN

Dr Shivkumar Kalyanaraman, CEO of the
Anusandhan National Research Foundation (ANRF),
visited IITGN on Aug 23, 2025, to share ANRF’s
vision for strengthening research in India. He spoke
about major grant programs, mission-driven
initiatives, and collaboration opportunities between
academia, industry, and government. Highlighting
the importance of interdisciplinary, outcomefocused work and emerging technologies like AI, he
also stressed inclusive, community-oriented
research.

IWATE VICE GOVERNOR VISITS IITGN

Mr SASAKI Jun, Vice Governor of Iwate Prefecture,
Japan, visited the IITGN campus along with officials
from JICA, Iwate Industry Promotion Center, and
GSEM on September 5, 2025. The visit focused on
collaborating in semiconductor research, skill

development, and innovation, with plans for
student exchanges, joint research, and industry
partnerships. Prof Rajat Moona, Director, IITGN,
emphasised the Institute’s leadership in AI and
integrated circuit design and its strong ties with
industry leaders like Micron, TATA Electronics, L&T
Semiconductor, and Infineon.

TRAINING PROGRAM ON
OPTIMISING POWER DESPATCH

IITGN held a one-day training program on
‘Optimising Power Despatch: SCED Initiative in
Gujarat’ on August 26, 2025, at the IITGN Research
Park. Organised by GETRI (a GUVNL subsidiary), the
event brought together experts from GUVNL and
its companies, GETCO, GSECL, and IITGN. Through
interactive sessions and hands-on exercises,
participants explored Gujarat’s power sector
roadmap, the Merit Order Despatch framework,
and advanced SCED optimisation using GAMS and
Python.

ODISI INITIATIVE SUPPORTED BY THE “ONE
SCHOOL AT A TIME” FOUNDATION

The ODiSI initiative has been launched with the
support of the “One School at a Time” Foundation,
under the leadership of Shri B V Jagadeesh and his
family. The initiative reflects a strong commitment
to advancing education and technological
awareness at the grassroots level. IIT Gandhinagar
continues to promote excellence in technology and
science education through impactful research,
education, and outreach. Shri Mayank Patel, a wellwisher from the USA, has also made a significant
contribution, strengthening academic resources
and expanding opportunities for students.

FERL AT IITGN HOSTS SHORT COURSE ON AI
IN FIRE ENGINEERING AND FORENSICS

The Fire Engineering Research Laboratory (FERL) at
IITGN successfully conducted a short course on AI
Applications in Fire Engineering and Fire Forensics.
The program provided participants with an
integrated overview of fire dynamics, explosions,
structural fires, forensic investigations, and the use
of artificial intelligence. Through interactive
lectures, FDS simulations, experiments, imagebased fire load analysis, and real-world case
studies, attendees gained both theoretical
knowledge and practical experience. The course
also emphasized the expanding role of AI in fire
modeling, image analysis, and forensic reporting
while addressing emerging challenges in the field.

IIT Gandhinagar

67

LABORATORIES
AND FACILITIES:

BIOLOGICAL SCIENCES
AND ENGINEERING
The department has made significant strides in
enhancing its research infrastructure, particularly
in molecular and cell biology, plant molecular
biology, biomaterials, drug delivery, and cell culture
technologies. The new equipment and upgraded
facilities will further support high-impact research
and interdisciplinary collaborations, reinforcing the
department’s commitment to scientific excellence.

ADVANCED TRANSGENIC GREENHOUSE
FACILITY: Tailored for plant biotechnology

research, IIT Gandhinagar (IITGN) hosts a state-ofthe-art transgenic greenhouse facility. The facility in
compliance with Department of Biotechnology
(DBT) guidelines ensures biosafety through design
features that prevent pollen escape and unintended
gene flow. It is fully automated with precise control
over temperature, light and humidity to mimic
optimal growth conditions. Standard Operating
Procedures (SOPs) align with DBT norms for
handling genetically engineered crop. Designed for
initial screening of transgenic events posttransformation, it supports cultivation of genomeedited crops like mustard and groundnut year-

round. This advances plant developmental biology,
genetics, and plant transgenics and CRISPR-based
genome editing studies at IIT Gandhinagar (IITGN).
MOLECULAR

BIOLOGY

LABORATORY

(MOBIL)

supports a wide range of research activities in
biochemistry, molecular biology, and cell biology. It
is equipped with advanced instrumentation,
including shaker incubators, laminar flow hoods,
sonicators, refrigerated centrifuges, a gradient
thermocycler, gel documentation systems, water
purifiers, ultralow and low-temperature freezers,
and a real-time thermocycler. Additionally, the lab
features a nano-drop UV-Vis spectrophotometer,
spectrofluorimeter, multimode microplate readers,
and a fast protein liquid chromatography (FPLC)
system, complete with a variety of columns for
comprehensive analysis.
CELL CULTURE FACILITIES (CCF) consists of three

fully-equipped cell culture laboratories, featuring
biosafety cabinets, CO2 incubators, centrifuges, an
automated cell counter, a sonicator, liquid nitrogen
cryopreservors, an inverted epifluorescence
microscope, and a multimode microplate reader
with AlphaScreen assay capabilities for highthroughput applications.

68

Annual Report 2025-26 | www.iitgn.ac.in

MACROMOLECULAR

AND

CHEMICAL

CRYSTALLOGRAPHY LABORATORY hosts a variety of

temperature-controlled incubators for crystal
growth and a stereomicroscope for screening of
the crystalline preparations.
PEPTIDE ENGINEERING AND PROTEOMICS (PEPR)

is equipped with state-of-the-art
instruments for the synthesis, characterization,
and analysis of peptides. This includes fume hoods,
manual peptide synthesis setups, semi-automated
peptide synthesizers, freeze dryers, analytical and
preparative HPLC systems, microplate readers,
centrifuges, ultracentrifuges, nanoparticle tracking
analyzers, ultra-microbalances, and refrigeration
units. Additionally, the facility houses a MatrixAssisted Laser Desorption Ionization-Time of Flight
Mass Spectrometer (MALDI TOF/TOF MS), complete
with
software
for
comprehensive
mass
characterization, sequencing, post-translational
modification (PTM) identification, comparative
proteomics, and polymer analysis. Associated
proteomics workflow instruments, including a
vacuum concentrator, 2D-gel electrophoresis
setup, sonicator, and centrifuge, are also available.
The facility is being further upgraded with the
support from the DST-FIST program through the
acquisition of an Ion Mobility-based high-resolution
mass spectrometer, enhancing our capabilities for
proteomics,
metabolomics,
and
lipidomics
research.

FACILITY

CLOCK LAB- The computational biology lab hosts a
high performance computing rack server with 64
cores, 512 GB RAM, NVIDIA A100 80 GB GPU and 20
TB Data Tier with containerized platform. Apart
from this there is a high end workstation with 48
cores, 128 GB RAM, NVIDIA RTX A5000 GPU and
16TB Data Tier. The lab also has five GPU
workstations and one desktop system for students
to work on simulations and data analysis. For data
backup and storage, a NAS has been installed with
a total capacity of 60 TB.
MEDICAL ULTRASOUND ENGINEERING (MUSE) LAB is
equipped with instrumentation dedicated for
innovating in tissue characterization and elasticity
imaging, molecular and contrast enhanced imaging,
ultrasound-mediated therapy, acoustic metrology
and sensing, and tissue-mimicking phantoms for
imaging and therapy. The facility has a
programmable research ultrasound imaging
system and equipment for generating and sensing
ultrasound fields ranging from 1 to 35 MHz, which
include an ultrasound beam mapping and

calibration system, ultrasound transducers,
hydrophones, pulser-receivers, arbitrary waveform
generators, digital and mixed signal oscilloscopes,
and power amplifiers.
is
equipped with BSL1+ facilities for the handling of
mesenchymal stem cells, primary cell cultures, and
tissues. The lab features a dedicated cell culture
area with two biosafety cabinets, CO2 incubators,
and a hypoxia chamber to support diverse
experimental conditions. The sample preparation
room is equipped with a PCR system, a small
microscope, and gel electrophoresis apparatus for
efficient sample processing and analysis.
Additionally, the lab is outfitted with chemical fume
hoods and specialized facilities for the synthesis
and characterization of a variety of nanomaterials,
including quantum dots and 2D and 3D
nanomaterials derived from biological sources,
enabling cutting-edge research in nanotechnology
and tissue engineering.

STEM CELLS AND TISSUE ENGINEERING LAB

PLANT MOLECULAR AND DEVELOPMENTAL CELL
BIOLOGY LABORATORY focuses on understanding
the cellular signaling events critical for successful
pollination. The laboratory is equipped with a
variety of advanced facilities, including PCR
machines, shaker incubators, laminar flow hoods,
electrophoresis units, UV transilluminators, micro
and mini centrifuges, pH meters, heat blocks,
dissection
microscopes,
an
epifluorescent
microscope, and plant growth chambers (PGC/S4L3, SR Lab Instrument and Percival) and greenhouse
facility, all supporting cutting-edge research in
plant molecular biology and development.
BIOMATERIALS AND DRUG DELIVERY LABORATORY is

equipped with advanced instruments for the
synthesis of materials, development of novel drug
delivery platforms, biomaterial characterization,
and research on antibacterial and mammalian cell
cultures. Key equipment includes shaker
incubators, laminar flow hoods, CO2 incubators,
centrifuges, pH meters, heat blocks, microscopes,
and other essential tools to support a wide range of
research and experimentation.
DNA SEQUENCING AND ANALYSIS FACILITY is
equipped with a MinION DNA sequencer from
Oxford Nanopore Technologies. This advanced
platform is capable of generating long-read
sequences, making it ideal for comprehensive
genomic and epigenomic analyses.

IIT Gandhinagar

CHEMICAL ENGINEERING

The Chemical Engineering Department has stateof-the-art laboratories, modern research facilities
and infrastructure. Over the years, the Department
has started working in diverse areas of research
that encompasses the core Chemical Engineering
problems as well as other emerging areas that
utilizes the principle of chemical engineering such
as nanotechnology, biochemical engineering, soft
and advanced materials, computational chemical
engineering, pharmaceutical engineering, and
environmental engineering etc. The Department
offers undergraduate programs (BTech), and
graduate programs (M. Tech and PhD). Many of the
research activities are funded by various
government agencies such as FIST Grant, DBT, DST,
DRDO,
CEFIPRA
(IndoFrench
Research
Collaboration) etc. The Department is also actively
working with various industries as part of industryacademia collaboration. Apart from the graduate
students, the undergraduate students working in
different research projects also avail this research
facility.

NEW FACILITIES ADDED

● Development of FIST Facility: As part of “Fund
for Improvement of S&T Infrastructure (FIST)
Grant”, the Department has recently procured
two high end equipment for this facility: Fourier
Transform Infrared Spectroscopy (Make:
BRUKER, FTIR with ATR and Accessories) and Gel
Permeation Chromatography (GPC: Waters
1500 series with Ri, UV and ELSD Detectors).
These equipment are used for characterization
of materials. This facility is not only open to IIT
Gandhinagar research community but also for
the researchers outside of the Institute.
● Development of Smart Materials and Green
Catalyst Laboratory facility: This facility was
earlier working in undergraduate laboratory.
However, it has recently found a new place in
AB5/205 as a new research facility. This facility is
being upgraded with newly installed state- ofthe-art research equipment and infrastructure.

MAJOR EQUIPMENT PROCURED

The Chemical Engineering laboratory has added
new research equipment and infrastructure during
this academic period. Some of these equipments
have already been installed, commissioned and are
contributing to the research activities, whereas the
rest are to be delivered soon. Among the major
equipments installed or ordered are:

COLLOIDAL ENGINEERING LABORATORY: The
laboratory is involved in active research in
nanoparticle synthesis, crystallisation, drug
polymorphism, and microbubble engineering for
pharmaceutical and biomedical applications. The
lab has a probe sonicator (Sonics VC 505), a particle

nm - 2 micron and particle sizing systems (PSSS)

probe (Kaiser USA, Model: RXN-1 785), solution
calorimeter (Paar USA, Model: 6755EE), water bath,
glass jacketed reactor, clean bench cabinet,
computer workstation, etc.
SOFT MATTER SCIENCE AND ENGINEERING
LABORATORY: The lab carries out experimental
research in stress and strain-controlled rotational
rheometer, optical microscope, instruments for
colloidal characterisation. The equipments in the
lab
are;
rheometer,
optical
microscope,
tensiometer, refrigerated and heated circulatory
bath (Model: IC301-K3), DLS and Zeta potential

70

Annual Report 2025-26 | www.iitgn.ac.in

measurement
instrument
(Brookhaven),
refrigerated tabletop centrifuge, a computer
workstation with servo stabilizer.
DRY PROCESS TECHNOLOGY (DRYPROTECH)
LABORATORY: The laboratory works on fine
particles and powder materials. The state-of-theart DryProTech Lab has several sophisticated
instruments such as surface energy analyser
(inverted gas chromatography), simultaneous
TGDSC from Netzsch, FT4 powder rheometer
(Freeman Technology), laser diffraction particle size
analyser (CILAS) for characterisation in dry and wet
mode. In addition, the lab is equipped with
V-blender and cone-mill (Prism Pharma), humiditycontrolled glove box and Faraday cup with
electrostatic charge measurement facility, planetary
ball mills, furnace chamber, tube furnaces, catalytic
reactor to study the performance of catalyst
powders, etc. The Lab also includes a skid-mounted
fabricated CO2 capture set up which can handle a
few grams of samples for CO2 sorption study.
FIRE RESEARCH LABORATORY: The laboratory is
exploring new technology for fire suppression and
fire retardant materials using various new
materials. The Laboratory has a cone calorimeter
(FFT, UK; Model: iCone mini), which is considered
the most significant bench-scale instrument in fire
testing. This apparatus has been adopted by the
International Organisation for Standardisation (ISO
5660- 1) for measuring heat release rate (HRR) of
materials under incident heat flux. The specimen
can be exposed to a maximum of 100 kW/m2 heat
flux. This device analyses the combustion gases
and measures the smoke produced from the test
specimen along with its time to ignition and
massloss rate. The data collected from this
benchscale test can be used for fire modelling,
prediction of real-scale fire behaviour, pass/fail
tests etc.
POLYMER ENGINEERING RESEARCH LAB (PERL):
The laboratory is involved in both experimental
and computational work in the areas of selfoscillating chemical reactions, shape memory
polymers, smart soft materials, polymer processing,
polymer gels and composites and pattern
formation. The laboratory is equipped with
computational facilities including state-of-the-art
workstations and access to high performance
clusters. The experimental facilities include
lyophilizer (Martin Christ, Alpha 2-4 LSC Basic) for
freeze-drying, centrifuge, refrigerated and heated

circulating cooling bath (IC 301- K3), bath sonicator,
magnetic stirrer (with temperature sensor), vacuum
pump, clean bench cabinet etc. We are in the
process of procuring a polymer extruder/mixer for
polymer blending polymers in pellets or powdered
form, hot press machine and a gel permeation
chromatography instrument.
COMPUTATIONAL
RATIONAL
DESIGN
LABORATORY: The laboratory is involved in
developing computational methods to rationally
design solid materials that show desired response
to the process conditions. The laboratory is
equipped with four high performance computer
workstations that are used to perform simulations
at the molecular and nano scales. The workstations
are equipped with the software to perform
molecular dynamics and Monte Carlo simulations
of biological and artificial systems, and equipped
with graphics processing units (GPUs) for the
visualisation of simulation-results. The workstations
are also used to develop and test the computer
programs used to perform advanced Monte Carlo
simulations.
CRTDH LAB: The CRTDH Lab is one of the state-ofthe-art facilities for chemical process and
wastewater related research. The laboratory is
equipped with sophisticated instruments like
inductively coupled plasma - mass spectrometry
(ICP-MS), optical emission spectrometry (ICP-OES)
for elemental analysis, total organic carbon (TOC)
analyser for carbon content, multi-mode plate
reader for microbial studies, fluorescence
spectrometer and UV-vis spectrophotometer for
determining optical properties of products and raw
materials,
high-performance
thin-layer
chromatography
(HPTLC)
for
compound
identification, gas chromatography for gas analysis
and spray dryer for liquid to solid processing in the
powder form, quick COD and fermenter, centrifuge
machine etc. The facility also has water quality
testing facilities like biological oxygen demand
(BOD), chemical oxygen demand (COD), total bound
nitrogen (TNb) and total dissolved solids (TDS).

SMART MATERIALS LABORATORY: The Smart
Materials Laboratory (SML) is exploring integration
of technologies from Chemical Engineering,
Materials Science and Electronics & Communication
to provide advanced solutions for multidisciplinary
projects. The SML is committed to gaining a
comprehensive understanding of the fundamental
science behind the behaviour of materials at
varying conditions. By leveraging the unique
properties of materials, SML is striving to create
innovative devices for sensing applications such as
wearable electronics, biosensors, controlled
farming, and microfluidic device fabrication
technologies.

In addition, the department has recently installed a
glove box, and a CHNS analyzer is expected to be
installed shortly. The laboratory infrastructure has
also been strengthened with the addition of hightemperature furnaces (up to 1000 °C), a quartz
tube vacuum-sealing station, and a bipotentiostat
with a rotating disk electrode (RDE) setup. These
instruments are used both for teaching and
research. The department also has a state-of-theart optical microscopy setup capable of imaging
single molecules and nanoparticles in confocal and
wide-field detection. We have also established a
dedicated biological laboratory facility equipped
with essential infrastructure, including a CO₂
incubator, fluorescence microscope, centrifuge,
−80 °C ultra-low temperature freezer, cell-counter,
cryocan for long-term sample storage of cells and a
biosafety cabinet, along with other needs to
support routine day-to-day biological and cell
culture requirements These facilities have
significantly enhanced the department's capabilities
in interdisciplinary areas covering chemistry,
biology, materials science, and nanophotonics.

GREEN CATALYSIS (GC) LABORATORY: The GC lab
focuses on catalyst design guided by in situ
spectroscopic techniques along with kinetic studies.
We aim to develop “green” catalysts that use
environmentally benign reactants for production of
value-added chemicals and fuels. A detailed
mechanistic
understanding
of
various
heterogeneous reactions is developed using
various batch and continuous reactors. Moreover,
we are equipped with tools for nanoparticle
synthesis, characterization, and kinetic studies.
Currently, we are in the process of establishing a
facility with a flow reactor connected to a Mass
Spectrometer to collect time-on-stream rate data.

GEOTECHNICAL ENGINEERING LABORATORY:
The Geotechnical Engineering laboratory is
equipped with high-end research equipment along
with basic soil testing. The soil dynamics laboratory
is equipped with large (earthquake) and small
strain (vibratory) testing. Large strain dynamic
loading: cyclic triaxial test setup (0.01 - 2 Hz, strain
controlled); electro-mechanical dynamic triaxial
test setup (0.01 - 10 Hz, stress and strain controlled,
K0, stress-path, user defined waveform, 10000
cycles running capability), cyclic simple shear setup
(0.001 - 5 Hz, stress and strain controlled) to
evaluate liquefaction, shear modulus & damping
ratio of soils up to 10,000 loading cycles. Small
strain dynamic loading: bender element system to
determine shear modulus under K0, stress path,
isotropic, UU, CU, CD compression and extension
loading conditions. Shear strength facility consists
of direct shear setup for cohesionless soils,
unconfined compression (UC) test for cohesive
soils, vane shear test for soft soils, and triaxial
setup with DAQ and analysis software for all soil
types. Pore pressure and volume change
measurement
facilities
are
available
for
compression/extension loading (UU, CU, CD tests),
K0 consolidation and stress path testing. Large

direct shear setup is also available to study the
interface behaviour of various types of
geosynthetics soil system. Dew point potentiometer
is available to determine total suction (0 - 300 MPa)
of fine-grained soils, conventional tensiometer for
coarse-grained soils, and filter paper setup for
matric suction of all soil types. The facility includes
falling and constant head devices for the
permeability of fine and coarse-grained soils, four
3-gang oedometer (1D consolidation) setup,
proctor setup, CBR for the strength of subgrade
soil, sieve shaker, hydrometer, Atterberg limit
(LL,PL,SL), swell pressure, specific gravity, relative
density, core cutter, sand replacement, muffled
furnace (900oC) for organic matter evaluation,
optical and digital LCD microscopes. The field
testing laboratory has plate load test of 300 kN
capacity with motorised anchoring system,
standard penetration test (SPT), dynamic cone
penetration test (DCPT) with automatic freefall
hammering system, vibratory plate compactor for f
ield compaction, field permeability setup, ground
penetrating radar (GPR) with monostatic and
bistatic operations facilitated with antennae of
frequencies 100 MHz, 400 MHz with the bistatic
operation and 200 MHz and 900 MHz with the
monostatic operation including 20-80 multi
frequency antenna, multichannel analysis of
surface waves (MASW) setup with the provision of
Seismic
Refraction/Reflection
Survey
and
Downhole/Crosshole
tests.
The
following
equipments were developed in the laboratory:
multiaxial cubical device with flexible boundary
system along with real time feedback control
system capable of conducting true-triaxial and
plane strain testing of soils, constant rate of strain
(CRS) consolidation setup, slurry consolidation
setup for preparing the remoulded specimens of
fine grained soils.
WATER RESOURCES ENGINEERING LABORATORY:
The Water Resources Engineering laboratory has
the following equipment for teaching purposes: a
hydraulic bench, pitot tube, Reynold’s apparatus,
sharp-crested weir (notch), Bernoulli’s apparatus,
venturi meter and orifice meter, nozzle meter,
hydraulic tilting flume, basic hydrology apparatus,
free and forced vortex f low apparatus. In addition
to the above, a river tray having levee breach
facility, an automated hydraulic tilting flume and a
piping system to study transients are being used
for research purposes. A 3D velocity measurement
device, Acoustic Doppler Velocimeter, is used in the
flume experiments.

IIT Gandhinagar

SURVEY AND GIS LABORATORY: The Survey and
GIS laboratory has been developed with the
procurement of various high-end survey equipment
and GIS software. Survey equipment includes
advanced integrated surveying kit which consists of
kinematic GPS, robotic total station and related
field and office software. It provides a common file
and user interface to GPS and total stations that
complement each other. Integrated surveying
provides a platform where GPS techniques can
extend a total station survey without the need for
extensive traversing. Besides this several total
stations, auto level, digital level and handheld GPS
are also procured, which will be used in addition to
the advanced integrated surveying kit. Multiuser
ArcGIS Info kit is procured to carry out GIS analysis
in teaching and research activities. ArcGIS package
will add the pre-existing image processing software
for handling the satellite data.
ENERGY, ENVIRONMENT, AND EXPOSURE
LABORATORY: The Environmental Engineering
and Exposure Laboratory (EEEL) has made
significant progress in the last academic year
towards advancing research, infrastructure, and
academic engagement. The laboratory expanded
its experimental capabilities by adding multiple
state-of-the-art instruments and facilities, including
a microplate reader, a thermomixer, a dual-channel
ambient dust sampler, a cascade impactor, and
real-time aerosol monitoring systems. The lab also
included a suite of environmental quality
instruments, such as Testo-based air velocity and
light meters, a suite of water quality instruments,
such as pH, dissolved oxygen, TDS/EC meters, a
photo colorimeter, and a turbidity meter. Life cycle
analysis tools were also integrated to support
sustainability assessment. The lab made significant
progress in the development and field deployment
of low-cost environmental quality monitors
(LCEQM) for broader application in urban health
and sustainability, with a focus on smart sensing,
hotspot identification, and intelligent building
management systems. One such application of
LCEQM included an intelligent AI-driven indoor
environment management system to protect
occupants’ health and comfort while minimizing
energy consumption, advancing sustainability, and
improving health in built environments. EEEL
actively contributed to research and academic
discourse by bringing together national and
international researchers, health professionals,
and climate scientists through symposia on air
pollution and its health impacts. The lab also

73

organized a focused group discussion on advancing
aerosol education and fostering the next generation
of a skilled workforce in aerosol science. At the
same time, students from the lab achieved
recognition on national platforms, including a topfive finish at the 28th Energy Technology Meet in
Hyderabad, and participated in international
research visits. Two of the lab members spent three
months each at the University of Illinois UrbanaChampaign, the University of Miami, and the
Missouri University of Science and Technology in
the USA. These visits were instrumental in
facilitating knowledge transfer and strengthening
international research collaborations. Ongoing
funded projects such as HEFA and SPARC at EEEL
continue to drive innovation in environmental
monitoring and exposure science.
TRANSPORTATION ENGINEERING LABORATORY:
The Department of Civil Engineering formally
inaugurated a new, state-of-the-art Transportation
Engineering Laboratory in November 2025. The Lab
is used for teaching UG and PG students. The lab
includes equipment for the testing of bitumen,
aggregates, and bituminous concrete for pavement
engineering applications. For bitumen testing, the
equipment includes a hot plate heater, a
penetration test, a softening point test, a Rolling
Thin Film Oven (RTFO), a pycnometer for specific
gravity, flash and fire point test (Cleveland cup
apparatus), a ductility test, and a Cannon-Manning/
Fenske viscometer. For aggregate testing,
equipment includes length gauges, an oven, sieves
and a mechanical sieve shaker, angularity number,
crushing and impact values, and the Los Angeles
Abrasion Test. For bituminous concrete testing, the
equipment includes a planetary mixer, a theoretical
maximum specific gravity test, a Marshall
compactor, a Marshall tester, and a centrifuge
extractor. Besides these, the Lab also has a skid
resistance meter for field evaluation of roads and
traffic counters and a radar speed gun for traffic
engineering applications.

The research facilities available at the cognitive

opportunity for interdisciplinary research and
collaboration. These facilities will help the
institution become a hub of research and
innovation, where students and faculty can work
together to solve some of the world’s most pressing
problems.

EARTH SCIENCES

Earth Sciences at IITGN envisions holistic
understanding of the Earth system through
multidisciplinary studies of its major components
like lithosphere, hydrosphere, atmosphere and
biosphere, and their interactions at different
spatiotemporal scales. We are dedicated to
conducting research and experiments not only
related to the study of these spheres but also the
processes that govern them. At IITGN, we currently
have Six Earth Science labs that are home to various
multi/ interdisciplinary projects and activities
starting from Earth surface processes, tectonic
geomorphology,
carbonate
sedimentology,
micropaleontology, stratigraphy, rock mechanics,

The Brain Stimulation facility includes a
Transcranial Magnetic Stimulation (TMS) system
and a neuronavigation system, which provides
precision targeting of single or repetitive magnetic
pulses to localize brain regions of stimulation.
Transcranial Direct Current Stimulation (tDCS) is
also used for non- invasive stimulation of the brain.

CO2 sequestration and geodynamics. These labs
are hubs of several international and national
collaborative projects funded by noted funding
agencies (such as MHRD, ANRF, DST, DST-UKIERI,
INSPIRE, MOES, MOEFCC, KPCSD, GSBTM). The lab
also promotes public/private partnership through
several tailor-made consultancy projects/services.

The Robotic system, digitising tablet, and motion
capture system are excellent tools for analysing
arm and hand movements. The robotic system
allows for simultaneous control of both robots for
comparison of inter-arm performance, making it an
ideal tool for studying bimanual coordination. The
digitising tablet records endpoint arm movement,
and the motion capture system provides a virtual
reality environment and allows the recording of
arm motion data under different task conditions.

The Workshop includes a sample preparation
facility to be analysed in major instruments. Earth
Science lab aims to prepare dry as well as wet
samples. The lab is equipped with rock crushing
and grinding facility, sieving, hand-held strong
magnetic separation. Furthermore, the Earth
Science discipline has started to develop a CORE
lab, which currently hosts cores that are used for
teaching as well as research related to paleoclimate
and subsurface reservoir studies. The lab currently
has a core plugging machine, and a hand-held drill
(with a maximum penetration up to 9 m). Most of
the questions being investigated in Earth Sciences
require field work. At IITGN the Earth Science
Discipline is currently working in large parts of the
Indian sub-continent specifically along various
River systems including Ganga, Yamuna (amongst
several others), as well as Basalts in Gujarat and
Maharashtra, hot springs in Ladakh, Cenozoic
lignite, shales and carbonates of Assam, Kutch,
Cambay, Barmer basins and modern carbonates in
Lakshadweep and Andamans.

The Behavioral Cubicles provide a soundattenuated
environment
for
conducting
experiments on decision making, attention, agency,
and other cognitive functions. The Psychophysiology
Lab is equipped with a wireless physiology-based
data acquisition system that facilitates real- time
data acquisition of physiological signals such as
ECG, EMG, and EDA.
The Multisensory Lab has a driving simulator built
entirely in-house for testing active noise cancellation
equipment with surround-sound speakers.
The Tactile Perception Lab features custom-built
Arduino-based devices supplemented by 3D
printing for conducting psychophysics experiments

The Archaeology Lab adopts an interdisciplinary,
science-based approach to study past human
societies through the analysis of material remains.
We examine archaeological artefacts to understand

IIT Gandhinagar

technological practices, raw material procurement,
and craft production. Methods such as microscopic
(using Leica Polarised and Stereo microscopes)
analysis,
mineralogical
and
geochemical
characterisation, and experimental approaches are
used to study material composition, function, and
use. This includes work on ceramics from sites such
as Karanpura, Kunal, Bhirrana, Ropar, Vadnagar,
and Vighakot; shell midden studies from Khadir
and Bela islands as well as archaeometallurgical
materials like zinc retorts from Zawar in Kachchh.
Geospatial tools, including GIS, remote sensing,
and GPS, are used alongside field and cartographic
data to support archaeological analysis. The lab
works on faunal remains to explore human-animal
relationships and subsistence systems. Faunal and
palaeopathological studies are carried out on
materials from sites such as Tigrana, Shikarpur,
Bagasra, and Loteshwar, as well as from the
Sanganakallu-Kupgal complex and Brahmagiri in
Karnataka. Taphonomic analysis is done on faunal
materials from Armenia. Biomolecular methods,
including
stable
isotope
analysis
and
palaeoproteomics, are also applied. Techniques
such as photogrammetry, 3D documentation, and
digital archaeology, along with ethnoarchaeological
perspectives, enable a balanced and integrated
understanding of archaeological materials and past
lifeways.
Field Survey and Core Description Lab in the
Department of Earth Sciences at IIT Gandhinagar is
equipped with essential field tools and a diverse
collection of core and hand samples. It is dedicated
to the systematic examination and interpretation
of both core and hand specimens. The lab focuses
on examining samples to understand rock types,
structures and textures with observations recorded
on log sheets. It is equipped with common field
instruments such as hammer, chisel, hand lenses
and measuring tools along with a drilling machine
capable of drilling up to 9 metres. In addition, the
lab utilizes drone surveys to collect high resolution
geological data including datasets from the Gulf of
Mannar and Palk Bay regions. Lab has four drones
(DJI MINI 2, DJI MAVIC AIR 2, DJI MAVIC 3 CLASSIC) all
equipped with RGB sensors as well as a Bajrang
drone fitted with a multispectral sensor. The lab
also maintains core samples from the Middle East,
Hawaii and Lakshadweep along with hand samples
from Andaman, Ladakh, Koyna and Kutch which
are used for study and interpretation.
Microscopy Lab the Microscopy Laboratory is a

75

state-of-the-art facility designed to support

imaging and documentation. Additionally, a

observations and teaching, and a Sharp
Dehumidifier ensures controlled environmental
conditions
for
maintaining
instrument
performance. Collectively, these facilities enable
polarized light microscopy, stereo microscopy,
fluorescence imaging, and digital documentation,
supporting applications such as geological thin
section
analysis,
mineral
identification,
microstructural studies, and academic training of
students.

COMPUTER VISION, IMAGING and GRAPHICS
(CVIG) LABORATORY: The Computer Vision,

applications. It also has GPU-enabled workstations
for deep learning research in computer vision and
will soon host a human motion capture system for
studying human dynamics.
PHOTONIC SENSORS LABORATORY: The Photonic
Sensors Laboratory at IIT Gandhinagar focuses on
near-IR and mid-IR tunable diode laser absorption
spectroscopy,
photoacoustic
absorption
spectroscopy, plasmonic nanobiosensing, microbial
growth studies, and fiber-optic biomedical
engineering. The lab is equipped with mid-infrared
quantum cascade lasers (Alpes Lasers), edgeemitting laser diodes (1392 nm, 1533 nm, 1650 nm
– Eblana, Toptica), a 100 mW, 4.3-4.7 µm quantum
cascade laser (Daylight Solutions), VCSELs (1278
nm, 2004 nm – Vertilas), and cooled and uncooled
photodiodes. It also houses a 50 MHz dual-channel
lock-in amplifier (Zurich Instruments), laser diode
current and temperature controllers (Thorlabs,
SRS), an arbitrary waveform generator (Agilent), a
500 MHz, 1 GS/s digital phosphor oscilloscope
(Tektronix), a digital delay and pulse generator
(SRS), and a 3 GHz spectrum analyzer (Agilent).
COMPUTATIONAL
NANOPHOTONICS
LABORATORY: The Computational Nanophotonics
Laboratory investigates the fundamental physics of
light interaction with nanostructured materials
with an eye towards applications in imaging,
sensing and energy harvesting. The myriad
applications that the lab investigates include highresolution and very wide field-of-view microscopes,
monolithic integrated ultraminiature cameras,
ultrasensitive non-destructive optical measurement
techniques for probing objects and dynamics at the
nanoscale,
bioinspired
sustainable
energy
harvesting and storage techniques. The lab is
equipped with broadband supercontinuum lasers,
UV-VIS NIR spectrometers and other tools for

77

characterisation of optical nanostructures and
metasurfaces.
ELECTRICAL
MACHINES
AND
POWER
ELECTRONICS LABORATORY: The Electrical
Machines and Power Electronics Laboratory at IIT
Gandhinagar is equipped for research on the
design, control, and diagnosis of electric machines,
including transformers, rotating machines, and
power converters. The lab utilizes 2D and 3D
electromagnetic finite element analysis in Ansys
Maxwell for the design and analysis of novel and
existing topologies. It features experimental setups
for permanent magnet brushless DC motors,
permanent magnet synchronous motors, and
switched reluctance motors. A unified test bench
supports the analysis of DC, induction, and
synchronous machines, while an eddy current
dynamometer enables motor loading and torque
waveform measurement. For machine health
diagnosis, the lab employs a precision magnetic
analyzer and an impulse generator for frequency
response analysis (FRA).
Recent additions to the lab include a Shunt Active
Power Filter, Precision Magnetic Analyzer, Eddy
Current Dynamometer, High Power DC Supply, and
Inverters, enhancing research capabilities in power
quality improvement, machine diagnostics, and
power electronics applications. The lab also
features programmable power supplies to simulate
balanced and unbalanced grid conditions, along
with fabricated power converters for AC-DC, AC-AC,
DC-AC, and DC-DC conversion. These resources
support advanced experimentation in electric
drives, energy conversion, and power electronics.
MEDICAL ULTRASOUND ENGINEERING (MUSE)
LABORATORY: The MUSE Laboratory is equipped
for advanced research in biomedical ultrasound
imaging, therapy, and metrology. The lab houses
single-element transducers (1-20 MHz), highintensity focused ultrasound transducers (2 MHz),
pulse receivers (1-50 MHz), an RF power amplifier, a
programmable ultrasonic data acquisition system,
and a broadband hydrophone. It also includes a
3-axis motorized acoustic beam mapping system, a
calibrated tissue-mimicking phantom, and a highend GPU workstation. Recent additions include a
refrigerator for biological sample storage. The lab
supports experiments with ex vivo tissues and
biological materials.
AUDIO SIGNAL PROCESSING LABORATORY: The
Audio Signal Processing Laboratory develops and

for audio devices such as active noise control
headphones, hearing aids, and hearables. The lab

45CA Ear Protector Test Module, measurement

SMART POWER ELECTRONICS LABORATORY: The
Smart Power Electronics Laboratory (SPELab)
focuses on developing efficient power electronic

NANOSTRUCTURE
AND
COMPOUND
SEMICONDUCTOR
DEVICES
LAB:
The
Nanostructure and Compound Semiconductor
Devices Lab (NCSD Lab) is dedicated to designing

MARITIME ENGINEERING

The Department of Maritime Engineering at Indian
Institute of Technology Gandhinagar is the maiden
initiative of its kind in the country, established to
address the evolving needs of the maritime sector
through high-quality education, research, and
industry collaboration. The department aligns with
national priorities such as port-led development,
capacity building, and the creation of maritime
clusters, thereby contributing to strengthening

India’s position in the global maritime ecosystem.
With increasing competition from global maritime
hubs, the department focuses on enhancing
technological
capabilities,
modernizing
infrastructure, and adopting international best
practices. It places strong emphasis on emerging
domains such as digitalization, artificial intelligence,
autonomous vessels, and green shipping practices,
ensuring the development of a future-ready and
resilient maritime workforce.
As a newly established department, continuous
efforts are being made to develop advanced
laboratories and infrastructure that support both
teaching and research activities. The department is
in the process of establishing modern facilities
focused on simulation-based learning, marine
systems, and maritime engineering applications.
These developing facilities are designed to provide
hands-on training, practical exposure, and
opportunities
for
applied
research.
The
infrastructure aims to support interdisciplinary
learning and foster strong industry engagement
through internships, collaborative projects, and
simulation exercises aligned with real-world
maritime challenges.

ACADEMIC AND RESEARCH FOCUS

The Department of Maritime Engineering at Indian
Institute of Technology Gandhinagar is focused on
developing strong academic and research
capabilities aligned with emerging trends in the
maritime sector. The department emphasizes
areas such as sustainable maritime infrastructure,
digitalization, artificial intelligence, and green
shipping practices.
In addition, it integrates interdisciplinary domains
including marine policy, logistics, and safety to
provide a comprehensive understanding of the
maritime ecosystem. The academic framework is
designed to combine theoretical knowledge with
practical exposure through simulation-based
learning, industry collaborations, and researchdriven initiatives.

PROGRAMMES

The department offers postgraduate and doctoral
programmes aimed at developing strong

maritime engineering. The curriculum is carefully
structured to cover both fundamental and
advanced areas, including advanced steel design,
environmental engineering, maritime logistics,
steam and gas turbines, and the analysis and
design of maritime structures.

surfaces.

Fuel Cell Systems Laboratory
Fuel Cell Systems Laboratory develops efficient,
silent and logistics-compatible fuel cell systems for
various applications in aerospace, stationary and
e-mobility by partnering with industry, government
and defense sectors. Currently, the lab is building
India’s first indigenously developed hydrogen
powered drone, while advancing the state-of-theart in fuel cell systems powered by logistic fuels
such as green-methanol and JP8/ military grade
diesel.
This year, the laboratory added an important new
facility: a 60 kW bi-directional power supply. This
power supply is synchronized with the building grid
and can upload power drawn during a fuel cell (or
battery) test directly to the building grid without
wasting this power. Further, the equipment is
capable of delivering 60 kW of power for battery
testing. This provides a significant boost to the
capability of the laboratory to develop and test fuel
cell systems for various applications. Further, the
laboratory has developed new test rigs for thermal
management, humidity management and passive
hydrogen recirculation, which are subsystems of a
liquid-cooled hydrogen fuel cell system. The
laboratory has also developed an on-board, ondemand hydrogen generator that can efficiently
generate hydrogen from solid chemical hydrogen
carriers.
FLUID MECHANICS AND HEAT TRANSFER LAB

This year Fluid Mechanics Lab has included various
new teaching apparatus including Flow meters in
series, Major & Minor loss apparatus, Bernoulli’s
apparatus and many others to provide better
understanding to students. These additions
enhance hands-on learning by allowing students to
conduct experiments on flow measurement,

IIT Gandhinagar

pressure distribution, and energy loss in pipelines.
The upgraded lab setup ensures a deeper
understanding of fluid behavior through practical
demonstrations,
complementing
theoretical
concepts. With advanced equipment and improved

learning experience, preparing students for realworld engineering challenges in fluid mechanics
applications. The lab also features wind tunnel and
KH instability setup which supports undergraduate

aerodynamic flow and visualization of instability
shear force in fluid. The Heat Transfer Lab also

further enhance the technical knowledge of fluid
mechanics and heat transfer, students were also
allotted self-project tasks.
Solid Mechanics LAB
In the past year, the Solid Mechanics lab contributed
for the upgradation of the Universal testing
Machine 10 Ton & Universal testing machine 20
Ton with the latest application software to provide
accurate readings for the sample testing. The lab
also features various experimental setups,
including bending moments, shear force, beam
deflection, bending stress, unsymmetrical bending,
buckling of struts, and indeterminate beams. These
rigs support in-class demonstrations and student
projects to enhance conceptual understanding. Key

Rockwell and Vickers hardness testers. Strain
gauges with data acquisition systems enable
hands-on experimentation, reinforcing practical
learning.
Manufacturing LAB
The Manufacturing Laboratory is equipped with

supports courses on manufacturing practices and
integrated design and manufacturing. Additionally,
the lab serves as a workshop for fabricating
undergraduate projects and research-related

machining and fabrication.
Robotics LAB
In recent years, robotics has gained significance in
both teaching and research at IIT Gandhinagar. As
programmable electro-mechanical systems, robots
integrate multiple disciplines, fostering interest

81

among students and faculty. The institute offers
several regular and elective courses, providing

programmed Quadruped Robot dog, Robotic
manipulator arm, Collaborative Robot and many
more. It also collaborated with elite organizations
including ISRO, DRDO, Indian navy for their
sponsored research projects.
Smart Energy and Thermal Transport Lab
The Smart Energy and Thermal Transport Lab
focuses on developing micro- and nanostructured
surfaces for controlling interfacial phenomena and
phase-change processes. The research integrates
fundamental investigations with application-driven
design, aiming to develop scalable solutions for
energy, thermal, and environmental systems. A
central theme of the lab is understanding how
surface architecture, chemical heterogeneity, and
liquid–vapor interactions govern wettability and, in
turn, fluid transport and phase-change behavior.
The lab emphasizes condensation heat transfer,
developing engineered surfaces that promote
sustained dropwise condensation and enhanced
performance under realistic conditions, including
the presence of non-condensable gases.
Complementing this, the group investigates droplet
impact and transport dynamics on tailored
surfaces, enabling control over spreading, rebound,
and adhesion.
These fundamental insights are translated into
diverse applications. The lab develops strategies
for frost inhibition and management in refrigeration
and heat pump systems using environmentally
friendly coatings. It also engineers antibacterial and
anti-biofouling surfaces, particularly for biomedical
devices, by leveraging surface structure and
chemistry to suppress bacterial adhesion and
biofilm formation. In parallel, bioinspired surface
designs are explored for improving droplet
interactions in agricultural spraying, enhancing
retention while reducing losses. The Lab also
contributes to emerging energy technologies,
including thermal management solutions for
electric vehicles and electronics cooling.
Dynamics and Vibrations Lab
The facility is equipped with whirling of shafts
setup, free & forced vibration setup, gear box
dynamics setup, static and dynamic balancing
setup, cam apparatus for teaching the role of static
and dynamic forces on different movable parts.
The research facility also consists of piezoelectric

82

Annual Report 2025-26 | www.iitgn.ac.in

sensors, including accelerometers and dynamic
force sensors of varying sensitivities and form
factors for diverse applications. It features
dedicated data acquisition hardware and software
for post-processing. Additionally, impact hammers
enable controlled modal and structural testing,
while a 1.6kN vibration shaker and a 200N modal
exciter, along with dedicated controller hardware
and software, support vibration testing. A highfidelity stroboscope facilitates structural dynamics
experiments, and high-precision surface plates
provide stable mounting for experimental setups.
Human-centered Robotics Lab
The Human-centered Robotics Lab is a research lab
as part of the Mechanical Engineering Department.
The lab is equipped with state-of-the-art facilities
for human motion measurement and analysis,
including motion capture systems, treadmills, force
plates, electromyography sensors, etc. The lab also
has robotic testbeds and exoskeletons. The lab
provides a unique opportunity for students to
conduct experiments in human biomechanics and
human-robot interaction and applications.

(CIF)

The research labs in the Physics Department are

Complex Fluids Lab. Dedicated research facilities

in the areas of Nanomaterials for energy research,

As part of our commitment to widespread
accessibility, users from around the country can
continuously avail CIF services through the National
I-STEM Portal. Furthermore, IITGN regularly
conducts workshops and training programs where
CIF staff and students provide targeted lectures on
the technical and applied aspects of our
instrumentation.

The Central Instrumentation Facility (CIF) at IITGN is
dedicated
to
providing
sophisticated
characterization services to internal researchers as
well as academic research institutes, universities,
and industries across India. Housed in its expansive
location at the new academic building (AB 13), the

environment to drive groundbreaking research and
innovation.

IIT Gandhinagar

NEWLY ADDED INSTRUMENTS

To keep pace with the rapidly evolving demands of
advanced research, the CIF has significantly
expanded its analytical capabilities. The following
state-of-the-art instruments have been successfully
commissioned this year:
X-ray Photoelectron Spectroscopy (K-Alpha XPS
System)
This advanced surface analysis technique provides
highly accurate quantitative and chemical state
information from the surface of solid materials. It is
critical for analyzing thin films, catalysts,
nanomaterials, and corrosion products.
Confocal-Raman Spectrophotometer
Combining high-resolution optical microscopy with
Raman spectroscopy, this system delivers detailed
molecular fingerprinting and chemical imaging. It
allows researchers to perform non-destructive,
high-contrast structural analyses of polymers,
biological tissues, and 2D materials.
High temperature Mechanical testing facility:
The High temperature mechanical testing facility
100SS-CF (from Zwick Roell Austria) can perform
room temperature tensile, compression and
3-point bending tests. It also has a high temperature
furnace which extends its capability for tensile, and
compression tests up to 1000 C. The closed loop
operation with extensometers further allows noncontact strain measurements for creep and stressrelaxation tests.

83

X-ray CT: The X-ray CT facility (Xradia 620 Versa)
can perform non-destructive inspection of internal
structure of solid material including internal crack
detection, crack propagation checkup, and porous
structure analysis with a resolution of up to 600
nm.
Cyclic
Voltameter
and
Electrochemical
Workstation (Pine Lab)
A high-precision platform designed for fundamental
electrochemical research. It is extensively used for
characterizing energy storage devices (like batteries
and supercapacitors), electrocatalysis, and studying
corrosion behavior.
Peptide Synthesizer (PurePep Chorus)
This automated, high-throughput synthesizer
accelerates biological and pharmaceutical research
by allowing researchers to synthesize custom, highpurity peptides efficiently. It features precise
fluidics and heating technology to handle complex
peptide sequences.
Super Resolution Microscope (Carl Zeiss)
Bypassing the traditional optical diffraction limit,
this advanced microscopy system enables
researchers to visualize cellular and molecular
structures at the nanoscale. It is highly sought after
for cutting-edge cellular biology and biophysics
research.
TIRF Microscope
Total Internal Reflection Fluorescence (TIRF)
microscopy restricts the excitation of fluorophores
to a very thin region near the specimen interface. It
is the gold standard for tracking single molecules,
studying cell membrane dynamics, and observing
viral infection pathways with minimal background
noise.

its

Circular Dichroism Spectrometer (Jasco CD1500)
Provides highly sensitive measurements of chiral
molecules, making it indispensable for structural

Elemental Analyzer (Elementar UNICUBE)

XRD (Bruker)
A modern X-ray Diffractometer to enhance our

NMR 600MHz (Bruker)

and

screening
capabilities
for

TOF HRMS (Bruker)
Time-of-Flight High-Resolution Mass Spectrometry
will enable exact mass determination and intricate
biomolecule profiling. It will be a cornerstone for
proteomics, pharmaceutical drug impurity profiling,
and complex mixture analysis.

LIBRARY
The Library, as a learning resource center being an integral part of the academic
and research work, continues to expand its collection both in print and digital form.
It also designs and delivers innovative services to support teaching, learning,
research, and other scholarly activities. During the reporting year, the Library has
initiated a number of important activities and services.

and research interests of the Institute. The table

access. However, they can add up to 10 students as
collaborators.
