INSTITUTE ARCHIVES

The Institute Archives made significant progress
during the period under report, in establishing a
structured, standards-based archival system. The
team developed extensive collections, including
3,838 digital items, 985 physical items, over 1,000
curated news records, and more than 100,000
photographs and videos. Additionally, 652
institutional milestones were documented, along
with 70,000 student activity records and
comprehensive award listings. Robust digital
infrastructure was implemented using Omeka,
ArchivesSpace, and DSpace. Policies, archival
frameworks, and metadata standards were
established, supported by global benchmarking
and research. Special collections, exhibitions, and a
dedicated physical space further strengthened
preservation, accessibility, and institutional
memory.

OUTREACH ACTIVITIES
Outreach Workshop for KGBV Educators: The
Library, in collaboration with the Centre for Creative
Learning (CCL), conducted an outreach session on
November 29, 2025 for educators from Kasturba
Gandhi Balika Vidyalaya (KGBV), Gujarat—
residential schools supporting girls from
disadvantaged
communities.
The
two-hour
workshop included activities like mind mapping,
DIY book creation, map-based reading, newsletters,
and storytelling, promoting low-cost library
initiatives.
Outreach for Shahpur School Students: On May
23, 2025, the Library, with NYASA, conducted an
outreach session for Adarsh Prathamik School,
Shahpur students. Held at the Library, it promoted
reading through print and digital resources.

Students explored tools such as Google Earth and
the National Digital Library of India, along with
translation aids, motivational videos, and activities,
highlighting the importance of public libraries and
community learning.
Winter Camp in collaboration with Nyasa and
Unnat Bharat Abhiyan: The Library organised a
winter camp for 8th-grade students from
government schools in the villages of Dashela and
Dharampur. 50 students participated in the session.
The programme covered basic library concepts,
types of libraries, and career opportunities in
librarianship. Further, students were introduced to
digital literacy concepts, with essential skills to
access and utilise information effectively.

NEW INITIATIVES

During the period of this report, the Library has
undertaken the following new initiatives.
Monthly Alert Services: The Library has introduced
several monthly alert services covering diverse
areas and topics for the IITGN community. These
services include The Wellness Shelf : Mental Health
& Well-Being, Innovate and Inspire: Entrepreneurial
Reads Collection – Library Alert Service, Interesting
Reads for Kids & Parents, Podcast Picks: Listen & Learn
– A Monthly Alert, Management Tips – Monthly Article
Alert Service.
Academic Writing Sessions: The Library conducted
structured sessions for students across three
courses—Introduction to Mathematical Writing (FP
602 - MA), Introduction to Physical Writing (FP 602
PH), and Introduction to Chemistry Writing (FP 602
CH). Six to eight sessions were conducted for each
course. The programme strengthened research
and scholarly writing skills through training in
library resources, literature discovery, citation
practices, Grammarly, Turnitin, and ethical
communication.
Brews and Chats (Open house with Research
Scholars): The Library hosted Brews and Chats, an
open-house series with research scholars to engage
with departments and better understand their
research support needs. During the period under
report, three sessions were organised with the
following departments: Electrical Engineering (1st
April), Chemistry (17th April), and Physics (16th
May).
Vendor Empanelment: The Library constituted a

IIT Gandhinagar

89

committee to empanel vendors for the supply of
books, print periodicals, e-resources, and e-books.
An Expression of Interest (EOI) was issued in

These were evaluated using defined eligibility and
scoring criteria. Based on the assessment, eight
vendors were shortlisted for book supply.
Additionally, four vendors each were recommended
for print periodicals and e-resources, and for
e-books, based on their eligibility, experience, and
overall performance.

DSpace-CRIS Implementation: The Library has
successfully
implemented
DSpace-CRIS,
a
comprehensive
repository
and
research
information management system designed to
showcase IITGN’s scholarly output. The upgraded
system integrates with external platforms to
automatically import publications, citations, and
relevant research metrics, including social media
indicators. It also enriches records by adding
publication venues and open-access links wherever
available. The repository hosts over 12,500
scholarly outputs, including 5,323 journal articles,
1,315 e-prints, 2,816 conference papers, and 1,524
M.Tech and Ph.D theses (full text available). It also
includes intellectual property records, comprising
41 published patents and 42 granted patents.

Digital Infrastructure: Digital infrastructure for
the Institute Archives was significantly strengthened
through the development of a dedicated website
and deployment of open-source archival platforms,
including Omeka, ArchivesSpace, and DSpace.
These systems enable efficient organization,
management, and access to archival materials. The
integrated infrastructure supports long-term
preservation and user-friendly discovery of
collections. The Institute Archives website can be
accessed at: https://institutearchives.iitgn.ac.in/

Repository link: https://repository.iitgn.ac.in/
Discovery Service: The Library has developed and
deployed VuFind, a unified search and discovery
platform that seamlessly integrates all library
resources into a single, user-friendly interface. This
platform enhances accessibility by enabling users
to search, browse, and filter across multiple
resource types simultaneously, including print
books, e-resources, subscribed databases, journals,
and institutional repository items. The system
supports seamless discovery across both print and
electronic collections, comprising 40,728 books,
18,444 journals, over 45 million journal articles, and
12,500 IITGN research publications. The discovery
service significantly improves resource visibility
and facilitates efficient and intuitive information
access for the IITGN research and student
community.

in/

ENHANCING LIBRARY WEB
INTERFACES AND DIGITAL
COLLECTIONS FOR IMPROVED ACCESS
AND LEARNING

Library web interfaces were modernized to create

dynamic, user-friendly platforms that enhance
accessibility and discoverability. Key pages—
including Course Reserve Books, E-Resources,
E-Journals, Periodicals Holdings, and Popular
Magazines—were redesigned with searchable and
interactive features. Simultaneously, digital

integration of 375 IEEE e-books into the Koha ILMS,

significantly improved resource visibility, navigation
efficiency, and support for teaching and learning
activities.

IIT Gandhinagar

CENTRES
ARCHAEOLOGICAL SCIENCES CENTRE

The Archaeological Sciences Centre (ASC) was
established in December 2012 to facilitate and
undertake research in the application of the
sciences in archaeology. The Centre also aims to
collaborate with universities and research
institutions, including the Archaeological Survey of
India (ASI) and State Departments of Archaeology,
to conduct such research.

CENTRE’S PROJECTS

● Mapping of Archaeological Heritage of South
Asia (MAHSA), funded by Cambridge University
under the MAHSA project, U.K., for a period of
4.5 years from September 2024 (PI: Prof V N
Prabhakar)
● Water Management during the Solankis: Analysis
of Water Bodies/Reservoirs and Religious
Structures, funded by Adani Enterprises for a
period of 5 years starting from December 2024
(PI: Prof V N Prabhakar)
● Towards a National Cultural Landscape
Information System: Mapping Heritage Sites for
Inclusive Planning, funded in collaboration with
NIAS, Bangalore (PI: Prof V N Prabhakar)
● Reconstruction of Human-Animal Interaction at
the World Heritage Harappan Site of Dholavira,
Gujarat, India: Inferences from the Isotopic
Composition of Archaeological Bone and Teeth
Remains, funded by DST SERB-POWER (PI: Prof
Sharada Channarayapatna and Co-PI: Prof
Debajyoti Paul, IIT Kanpur)
● Paleo-landscape, Paleo-drainage and Potential
Groundwater Investigations in the Great Rann of
Kachchh, Gujarat, Western India, funded by the
Ministry of Earth Sciences, Government of
India, for a period of 3 years (starting April 2023)
(PI: Prof Debajyoti Paul, IIT Kanpur and Co-PI:
Prof V N Prabhakar)
● Setting up of the Indian Knowledge System Cell
at the Archaeological Sciences Centre, IIT
Gandhinagar, funded by the IKS Cell, Ministry
of Education, Government of India (PI: Prof V
N Prabhakar and Co-PIs: Prof Manish Jain and
Prof Mana Shah)

DESIGN AND INNOVATION CENTER

Design at IIT Gandhinagar has evolved over more
than a decade through the Design and Innovation
Centre established in 2014. During this time, design
has been integrated into courses, studios, and
interdisciplinary projects across the institute. DIC
has also been engaging the community in
extracurricular activities including talks, seminars,
symposiums, and workshops.
Approaches such as entrepreneurship, systems
thinking, sustainability, and hands-on making have
been central to the courses offered under design.
This has allowed design to function not as an
isolated discipline, but as something embedded
within engineering and scientific contexts.

IIT Gandhinagar

thinking and prototyping.

a project titled “Designing social robot
interactions to support learning in collaborative
making tasks”. There are currently three
students and staff working on the project with

system to support students as they work on
making tasks.

DR KIRAN C PATEL CENTRE FOR
SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT

The Dr Kiran C Patel Centre for Sustainable
Development (KPCSD) at IIT Gandhinagar (IITGN)
undertakes cutting-edge research on sustainability
and related challenges of high societal importance
and promotes cost-effective and sustainable
solutions through its strong outreach and
technology-transfer programmes.

PROGRAMMES
Research

The Centre advances interdisciplinary research on
sustainability challenges of national and global
relevance. It fosters collaborations with academic
institutions, government agencies, and international
partners to generate impactful knowledge and
innovative solutions. The research focus extends
from fundamental inquiry to applied problemsolving aimed at societal transformation.
Practice
The Centre promotes lab-to-field translation of
sustainable technologies and solutions within the
campus and surrounding communities. By
positioning the campus as a living laboratory, it
supports pilot implementation, demonstration
projects, and scalable sustainability interventions.
These efforts ensure that research outcomes
translate into tangible environmental and social
benefits.
Education
The Centre integrates sustainability into academic
curricula and nurtures interdisciplinary learning
across engineering, sciences, humanities, and
policy. It supports courses, seminars, student
projects, and experiential learning opportunities to
build sustainability literacy. Through national and
global engagement, it contributes to advancing
sustainability education beyond the institute.
Outreach
The Centre actively organizes conferences, lecture
series, workshops, and networking platforms that
connect scholars, professionals, policymakers, and
industry leaders. It promotes dialogue, knowledge
dissemination, and collaborative engagement on
emerging sustainability issues. Outreach initiatives
strengthen partnerships and enhance public
awareness of sustainable development.
Capacity Building
The Centre designs and facilitates customized
training programmes, fellowships, and collaborative
platforms
to
strengthen
sustainability
competencies. It encourages interdisciplinary
knowledge sharing and provides access to
resources and opportunities for students,
researchers, and practitioners. Through these
efforts, it builds long-term institutional and
community capacity for sustainable development.

CENTRE FOR RESEARCH
COMMERCIALIZATION (CRC)

The Centre for Research Commercialization (CRC),
established in early 2025, serves as a dedicated
hub to bridge the gap between academic
innovation, industry requirements, and market
readiness. CRC adopts a data-driven approach to
analyse academic expertise, emerging trends, and
industry needs. It aims to accelerate industry–
academia collaboration and support stakeholders
in transforming research prototypes into
commercially viable products, while also
contributing to policy development aligned with
government priorities and national interests.
Year One at a Glance
● 14 months of operation
● 10+ data sources integrated
● Coverage across CFTIs (Centrally Funded
Technical Institutions)
● 1 strategic partnership
Key Achievements in Year One
● Core Team and Infrastructure Established:
CRC developed its foundational operational
capacity within the first year, including the
establishment of a core team and initial
infrastructure based on requirement analysis.
● Framework for Multi-Source Data Collation:
A robust framework has been created to
integrate data from multiple sources. This

includes intelligent automation for data
extraction, transformation, curation, and
validation, tailored to the needs of CFTIs.
● Academic
Program
Data
Aggregation:
Comprehensive data on academic programs,
outputs,
Entrepreneurship
Development
Programs
(EDPs),
Faculty
Development
Programs (FDPs), and institutional expenditure
has been systematically collated across CFTIs.
● Launch
of
Data
Portal:
A data portal with interactive dashboards has
been developed, enabling temporal analysis,
benchmarking,
and
cross-institutional
comparisons over a five-year period.
● Strategic
Partnership
with
BISAG-N:
CRC established a collaboration with the
Bhaskaracharya Institute for Space Applications
and Geoinformatics (BISAG-N) to co-develop the
knowledge base and data portal infrastructure.

KNOWLEDGE BASE: DATA DOMAINS

The CRC knowledge base integrates structured
data across six key dimensions:
● Faculty and Expert Competencies: Skills
mapping across disciplines and research
domains
● Research Funding: Grants and financial
resources tracked across institutions
● Intellectual Property and Research Publications:
Patents and scholarly outputs
● Startup Ecosystem and Research Projects:
Incubated startups, consultancies, and
innovation activities
● Academic Programs and Outputs: Programs,
EDPs, FDPs, student demographics,
placements, and higher studies
● Institutional Financials: Capital and operational
expenditures

PARTNERSHIP SPOTLIGHT: BISAG-N

CRC has partnered with the Bhaskaracharya
Institute for Space Applications and Geoinformatics
(BISAG-N) to jointly design and develop the
knowledge base and interactive data portal. This
collaboration leverages BISAG-N’s expertise in
geospatial technologies and large-scale government
data systems to strengthen CRC’s commercialization
ecosystem.

LOOKING AHEAD

With its foundational systems in place, CRC is wellpositioned to expand its impact. Key priorities
include:

IIT Gandhinagar

● Expanding the knowledge base to cover more
institutions with greater data granularity
● Enhancing accessibility of the data portal for
stakeholders and decision-makers
● Incorporating user feedback mechanisms for
continuous improvement
● Strengthening infrastructure to handle larger
data volumes and advanced automation
● Developing advisory and policy outputs based
on data-driven insights
● Formalising additional industry–academia
collaborations to accelerate the transition from
prototype to product

CENTRE FOR SAFETY ENGINEERING

The Centre for Safety Engineering (CSE) and Fire
Engineering Research Laboratory (FERL) at IIT
Gandhinagar are dedicated to enhancing public
safety by addressing critical challenges and
developing innovative solutions. With a strong
focus on national priorities, CSE actively supports
Indian manufacturers through collaborative
research projects involving industry, government,
and academia.

SHORT COURSE:

AI Applications in Fire Forensics: With AI
increasingly shaping various industries, fire safety
is no exception. CSE and FERL organised a short
course on AI Applications in Fire Engineering and
Fire Forensics from Dec 09-13, 2025. The course
featured cutting-edge AI applications in fire
modelling, evacuation planning, and compartment
fire analysis. Esteemed faculty and industry experts
facilitated a broad spectrum of learning
experiences, ensuring participants gained valuable
insights.

FIRE FORENSIC INVESTIGATION:

MRF, a premier tire manufacturing company, has
engaged CSE/FERL to investigate a Fire/Explosion at
its manufacturing plant in Dahej.

CSE AND FERL AT FSBI 2025 EXHIBITION:

CSE and FERL, a knowledge partner of the Fire Safe
Build India (FSBI), participated in the FSBI Exhibition
in Mumbai from Dec 04-05, 2025. Dr Dravesh Yadav
emphasised the importance of passive fire
protection and presented research and testing
capabilities of FERL. Professionals from government
institutions, independent consultancies, research
institutes, and corporations attended the event.

EXTERNAL
AFFAIRS
MoUS

IITGN has been steadily strengthening strong and mutually beneficial relationships with
internationally renowned academic institutions and non-academic organisations. In 2025–26, several
partnerships were formed, benefiting both students and faculty.

IITGN SIGNS MoU WITH CPWD

IITGN signed a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) with the Central Public Works Department (CPWD)
on April 7, 2025, in New Delhi. The focus of the collaboration is on joint research and innovation in Building
Construction Technology, with the goal of enhancing the quality, efficiency, and sustainability of public
infrastructure. The partnership will explore the application of emerging technologies such as Artificial
Intelligence (AI), Internet of Things (IoT), Building Information Modelling (BIM), and green construction
methods.

IITGN SIGNS MoU WITH IACE

IITGN has entered into a strategic partnership with the International Automotive Center of Excellence (iACE)
through the signing of a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) on May 29, 2025. The partnership aims to
foster joint research projects that address real-world industrial challenges, develop integrated training
programs and hands-on workshops, and enable shared access to laboratories for innovation and product
development. It also seeks to explore emerging technologies such as electric vehicles, hybrid systems, and
advanced automotive solutions.

semiconductor ecosystem, and advance the Make
in India vision for critical technologies. The
agreement includes setting up a dedicated research
centre at IITGN with a five-year plan to drive
innovation in secure computer technologies.

IIT GANDHINAGAR INNOVATION AND
ENTREPRENEURSHIP CENTER
IITGN Innovation and Entrepreneurship Center (IIEC) is a technology business incubator to support the
incubation and technology commercialisation initiatives of the Institute. It aims to foster technoentrepreneurship through innovative and creative thinking, using an interdisciplinary approach. This year,
the IIEC supported a total of 100 startups, fostering innovation and entrepreneurship across various
domains.

Research Park. The workshop brought together 40
professionals from GUVNL, PGVCL, MGVCL, UGVCL,

VENTURE SPARK PROGRAM – MODULE
1: IDEATION AND PRODUCT
OPPORTUNITY

The Venture Spark Program was conducted in four
modules during 2025 at the Research Park, IIT
Gandhinagar, to support early-stage entrepreneurs
in transforming ideas into scalable ventures.
Module 1: Ideation and Product Opportunity was
held on July 24, 2025, and led by Mr. K. Thyagarajan,
focusing on identifying business opportunities,
understanding customer needs, and developing
value propositions.
Module 2 was conducted on September 2–3, 2025,
and delivered by Mr. Rajeev Tandon and Prof
Sanjay Dwivedi, providing hands-on insights into
venture building and growth strategies.
Module 3, held on October 10, 2025, emphasized
validating ideas, refining revenue models, and
developing sustainable growth strategies, with
participation from 18 attendees.
Module 4: Financial Planning and Management was
conducted on November 19, 2025, and led by Mr.
Praveen Gupta, focusing on financial strategy and
operational efficiency, and was also attended by 18
participants.

A one-day training program on “Optimizing Power
Despatch: SCED Initiative in Gujarat” was conducted
on August 26, 2025, at the Research Park, IIT
Gandhinagar. Organized by GETRI, a subsidiary of
GUVNL,
the
program
brought
together
professionals from GUVNL and its subsidiary
companies, along with Prof Naran Pindoriya and
his students from IITGN. Through interactive
sessions and hands-on exercises, participants
explored Gujarat’s power sector roadmap, the
Merit Order Despatch framework, and advanced
SCED optimization using tools such as GAMS and
Python, fostering meaningful discussions and
knowledge exchange.

SMART INDIA HACKATHON 2025

The Smart India Hackathon (SIH) 2025 was
organized on September 29, 2025 at the Research
Park, bringing together students to address realworld challenges through innovative solutions. As a
premier nationwide initiative, SIH fosters creativity,
collaboration, and practical problem-solving by
engaging participants with industry experts,
government bodies, and other stakeholders. The
event provided a dynamic platform for students to
showcase their ideas and develop impactful
solutions. A total of 79 participants took part in the
hackathon.

VIDYUTAI HACKATHON 2025

VidyutAI Hackathon 2025, a 24-hour AI-for-energy
hackathon, was organized on October 5–6, 2025 at
the Research Park by SPEL @ IITGN. The event
received 60 applications, out of which 16 teams
were shortlisted to participate. The hackathon
focused on developing innovative solutions in
areas such as EV safety and smart energy
management. It provided a platform for participants
to work intensively on real-world challenges,
fostering innovation and collaboration. The event,
held from 9:00 AM to 4:00 PM, saw participation
from 51 attendees.

IGNITE 2025: IDEATION AND VENTURE
BUILDING WORKSHOP

A two-day hands-on workshop, Ignite 2025, was
conducted on January 17–18, 2025 focusing on
ideation and venture building, and attended by 40
participants. Delivered by domain experts, the
programme covered key aspects such as developing
research-driven ideas to address industry
challenges, idea research and aspiration mapping,
idea–solution alignment, challenge validation
frameworks, and strategic team building.
Participants also engaged in exercises on striving
and discoveries, along with 1:1 mentoring sessions
aimed at refining their ideas. The sessions were led
by Mr. Kiriti Kapavari, an entrepreneur and startup
mentor specializing in early-stage venture building
and market discovery, and Mr. Suresh Narasimha,
a seasoned entrepreneur, venture capitalist, and
Co-creator of CoCreate Ventures, who supports
deep-tech innovations through the Aarambh VC
fund.

WORKSHOP ON MULTI-YEAR TARIFF
(MYT) FRAMEWORK

A one-day workshop on the Multi-Year Tariff (MYT)
Framework in Gujarat – Batch 02 was conducted on
September 16, 2025, at Classroom 107, Research
Park, IIT Gandhinagar. Organized by GETRI, a
subsidiary of GUVNL, the workshop brought
together professionals from GUVNL and its
subsidiary companies, including PGVCL, MGVCL,
UGVCL, DGVCL, GETCO, and GSECL. The session
facilitated in-depth discussions and knowledge
sharing on regulatory frameworks, tariff structuring,
and sustainable energy economics, contributing to
capacity building in the power sector.

VISIT BY GOVERNMENT SCIENCE
COLLEGE, GANDHINAGAR

A visit by Government Science College, Gandhinagar
was organized on September 26, 2025, at the

IIT Gandhinagar

Research Park, where 2 faculty members and 31
students participated. The visit aimed to create
awareness about the startup ecosystem and
emerging opportunities in innovation and
entrepreneurship. Participants were introduced to
the functioning of startups and gained practical
insights into entrepreneurial processes through
their interaction and exposure at the campus.

FUNDRAISING SESSION WITH CII AND
PARUL INNOVATION CENTRE

A session on “Mastering Fundraising for Startup
Growth” was conducted on October 10, 2025 at the
Parul Innovation Centre, Vadodara, in collaboration
with IIT Gandhinagar–IIEC and the Confederation of
Indian Industry (CII). The session, attended by 46
participants, was led by Mr. Anand Pandey,
Incubation Manager at IITGN Innovation and
Entrepreneurship Center, and Mr. Prashant Gupta,
Deputy Director & Head, CII Central Gujarat Zone. It
provided founders with valuable insights into
investor relations, funding stages, and effective
pitch-building strategies, equipping them to
navigate the startup fundraising landscape more
effectively.

FINNOVATE HACKATHON 2025

Finnovate Hackathon 2025, a 24-hour fintech
hackathon, was organized by the IITGN Innovation
and Entrepreneurship Centre (IIEC) on November
7–8, 2025 at the Research Park. The event brought
together young innovators from across Gujarat to
reimagine the future of finance. The hackathon was
inaugurated by Prof Rajat Moona, Director, IITGN,
along with leadership from Adani Enterprise.
Participants worked on real-world problem
statements using authentic datasets, guided by
mentors from academia and industry. Teams from
institutions such as SVNIT, PDEU, IIIT Vadodara,
GECs, and GTU showcased remarkable innovation
and collaboration. The event served as a catalyst
for future-ready fintech solutions and strengthened
industry-academia engagement. A total of 86
participants took part in the hackathon.

IIT GANDHINAGAR RESEARCH PARK
The IIT Gandhinagar Research Park seeks to push the boundaries of innovation and research by fostering a
strong connection between industry and academia. Industries can set up their offices at the IITGN Research
Park to carry out R&D activities. This allows them to be part of a vibrant community and gain access to R&D
professionals, students, and state-of-the-art R&D infrastructure at IITGN.

NEW COMPANIES

During this period, the Research Park further strengthened its industry engagement with the induction of
five new companies—Firelynk Protech Limited, Specific Fire Protection Limited, SPEC Finance Private Ltd.,
Electrobotic Manufacturing Pvt. Ltd., and Electronics Corporation of India Ltd. (ECIL), along with Harsha
Engineers.

EVENTS AND ACTIVITIES
GEZIA DELEGATION VISIT TO IITGN RESEARCH PARK

A delegation from GEZIA, comprising 28 representatives, visited the IIT Gandhinagar Research Park on
January 28, 2026. During the visit, the delegation was introduced to the R&D facilities at IITGN and engaged
in meaningful interactions with faculty members. The visit also included an interaction session and a Special
Interest Group (SIG) meeting, aimed at exploring potential opportunities for collaboration in research and
innovation.

VISIT OF STUDENTS FROM TKM COLLEGE OF ENGINEERING, KERALA

Students and faculty members from TKM College of Engineering, Kerala visited the IIT Gandhinagar Research
Park on August 29, 2025. During the five-hour visit, 110 participants explored the Namtech Labs and
interacted with startup companies based at the Research Park, gaining valuable insights into innovation,
research, and industry practices.

VISIT OF JAPANESE DELEGATION – INDIA–JAPAN TALENT BRIDGE SEMINAR

The India–Japan Talent Bridge (IJTB) Career Seminar was held at IIT Gandhinagar on 30th August 2025,
bringing together delegates from six leading organizations, including Beyond Next Ventures, Dai-ichi Life
Techno Cross Co., Ltd., Members Co., Ltd., Money Forward India, Musashi Seimitsu Industry Co., Ltd., and
Nidec Motor Corporation. The seminar provided valuable insights into career opportunities, industry
expectations, and pathways for strengthening professional connections between India and Japan. Aligned
with the India–Japan Action Plan, the initiative aims to facilitate large-scale talent exchange between the two
countries.
During the three-hour visit, around 60 participants engaged in enriching interactions, gaining exposure to
global work culture and innovation-driven industries. The delegation also toured the IITGN campus and
Research Park, interacted with faculty, researchers, and students, and explored potential avenues for future
collaboration.

YEAST ERA COMPANY MEETINGS

IIT Gandhinagar Research Park hosted business meetings with the Yeast Era Company from Vietnam on
August 8 and 11, 2025, focusing on exploring opportunities in biotechnology and fermentation-based
innovations. The discussions involved a 15-member delegation representing five companies and facilitated
a vibrant exchange of ideas around joint research, technology transfer, and business expansion. These
interactions strengthened the Research Park’s international outreach and highlighted its commitment to
fostering a global ecosystem for research and industrial collaboration.

IITGN FEATURED IN INDIA TODAY-MDRA TOP
ENGINEERING INSTITUTES LIST 2025

IIT Gandhinagar (IITGN) has achieved outstanding
recognition in the India Today–MDRA Survey 2025.
The Institute has been ranked 1st among the Top
10 Emerging Government Engineering Colleges in
India, underscoring its commitment to academic
excellence and innovation. IITGN has also climbed
to the 8th position in the list of Top 10 Engineering
Colleges of 2025, a significant leap from its 16th
rank in 2020—making it the most improved
institution in this category. Additionally, IITGN
secured the 2nd rank among the Top Three Regionwise Engineering Colleges in Western India. These
achievements reflect the Institute’s relentless
pursuit of excellence in education and research.
(India Today–MDRA Survey 2025–26, Volume XVIII,
Issue I, July–September 2025)

campus practices.

ACTIVITIES
NEEV: IIT GANDHINAGAR’S
COMMUNITY OUTREACH PROGRAMME
NEEV, the community outreach wing of IIT Gandhinagar, empowers women and youth from under-served
communities through skills development, education, and livelihood programs. Since its inception in 2014,
NEEV has conducted projects and activities impacting 10,000+ beneficiaries from the areas surrounding IIT
Gandhinagar including several nearby villages.
In the year 2025–26, NEEV IIT Gandhinagar structured its interventions across three key verticals to create
holistic community impact:

IIT Gandhinagar

121

SKILLS, ENTREPRENEURSHIP DEVELOPMENT AND AWARENESS

During 2025-26, NEEV organized 23 skill development and entrepreneurship courses, and 12 awareness
activities for 1910 beneficiaries from 32+ villages including Palaj, Basan, Chiloda, Dabhoda, Prantiya, Magodi,
Ratanpur, Kolavada, Alampur, Dholakuva, Lekawada, Pethapur, Chala, Lavarpur, Nava Dharampur, Borij,
Mota Isanpur, Rampura, Chandrala, Dholarana Vasna, Dhanap, Shahpur, Vadodaragam, Vavol, Bhundiya,
and nearby peri-urban areas in Gandhinagar and Ahmedabad cities. There was an 80% course completion
rate across all programs, and 70% of skilled participants are utilizing their training for employment,
education, or entrepreneurship.
These courses and sessions were conducted in partnership with The Desai Foundation Trust, Milacron India
Pvt. LTD and Google Research (for computer related courses).

STITCHING SKILLS TRAINING COURSE

With a focus on empowering rural women, NEEV conducts sewing training courses in various villages. The
modules include measurement, marking, cutting and sewing various products such as cushion covers,
cloth bags, baby frocks, salwar-kameez, and ladies' blouses. Four batches of sewing courses were conducted
in 2025-26:

at Chhala Village for 30 women.

IIT Gandhinagar

123

EDUCATION LIVELIHOOD VERTICAL

As part of the Livelihood vertical, NEEV facilitated income-generation opportunities for women through
production-based activities and the fulfilment of bulk orders of stitched products such as tote bags, laptop
bags, and sling bags, from both on-campus and external organisations. Key external collaborations included
organisations such as RIS, Sports Authority of Gujarat, GUJCOST, and IEEE which provided significant order
opportunities.
During the year, 20+ women artisans actively contributed to these assignments and received direct
livelihood earnings based on their work participation. Individual yearly incomes ranged from approximately
₹15000 to ₹82,000, with several participants demonstrating high engagement and consistent productivity.
The earnings reflect the effectiveness of the livelihood model in generating sustainable income opportunities.
Beyond financial support, the initiative has strengthened women’s skills utilisation, confidence, and active
participation in economic activities, contributing to long-term livelihood security and community
development.

OTHER NOTABLE EVENTS

Navratri Mahila Haat (September 2025): Organized during the festive season, this event brought together
women entrepreneurs to exhibit traditional wear, handicrafts, and other products, enabling market access
and income generation.
Campus and Library Exposure Visits: NEEV organized campus and library visits for students from
Industrial Training Institute Gandhinagar, and Industrial Training Institute Kubernagar, providing the visitors
exposure to the IIT Gandhinagar environment, learning resources, and academic ecosystem.

NYASA: IITGN’S
COMMITMENT TO SOCIAL
OUTREACH
SUMMER CAMP 2025

Nyasa, in collaboration with the Desai Foundation
Trust, successfully organised a ten-day Summer
Camp from May 21 to 30, 2025, with participation
from over 50 students from Shahpur Adarsh
Primary School and more than 20 Nyasa students.
The camp featured a multidisciplinary curriculum
designed to foster creativity, innovation, and
holistic development.
Participants engaged in Creative Arts and Heritage
activities, including calligraphy, superhero costume
design, and an immersive archaeology workshop
involving fossil excavations. STEM-focused sessions
conducted by CCL, Tinkerers’ Lab, and Maker
Bhavan introduced students to hands-on learning
through 3D printing, laser cutting, and experiments
with non-Newtonian fluids. The camp also
emphasised holistic well-being through sports and
expert-led sessions on gender sensitization and
menstrual hygiene. In addition, initiatives by the
Green Club and IITGN Library promoted
environmental awareness and digital literacy.
The camp concluded with student-led film
screenings and the felicitation of volunteers,
marking a successful and enriching experience
centred on curiosity, learning, and collective
achievement.

INDEPENDENCE DAY

The Nyasa community celebrated the 79th
Independence Day on August 15, 2025, with a
strong sense of pride and national spirit. The event
featured heartfelt patriotic performances by
students, whose songs and expressions reflected
the values of freedom, unity, and collective identity.
A key highlight of the celebration was the tradition
of expressing gratitude through the felicitation of
the previous year’s Chetana and Akanksha
volunteers,
recognising
their
invaluable
contributions to the school’s mission. The
celebration, marked by both cultural expression
and formal acknowledgement, reflected the vibrant
and collaborative spirit that defines Nyasa.

TEACHERS’ DAY &
CHILDREN’S DAY CELEBRATIONS

Nyasa celebrated Teachers’ Day with a focus on

gratitude and the transformative role of educators.
The programme commenced with soulful prayers
and songs led by student volunteers, followed by
inspiring addresses from Falguni Ma’am and Faculty
In-Charge, Prof. Krista R. Khiangte. The celebration
also included group activities to foster camaraderie,
a formal felicitation of Nyasa members and
supporters, and concluded with a cake-cutting
ceremony and cultural performances.
Children’s Day was observed in collaboration with
the IIT Gandhinagar Counselling Team through a
series of interactive sessions aimed at building
confidence and encouraging self-expression
among students. These engaging activities provided
a supportive environment for children to explore
their creativity and imagination, while also
contributing to their social and emotional wellbeing.

GANESHOTSAV AND
ONAM CELEBRATIONS

Nyasa celebrated Ganeshotsav on August 30, 2025,
in collaboration with Team Ganesha, offering
students a rich cultural and creative experience.
The celebration included a two-part Ganeshamaking workshop conducted by Mr Dishant
Tanmay of Palette, along with an engaging
storytelling session by Mr. Nivid Desai that
captivated the young audience. This was followed
by the celebration of Onam on September 5, 2025,
where students participated in traditional activities
such as creating intricate Pookalams and
experiencing the festive Onam Sadya served on
banana leaves. These celebrations provided
students with meaningful exposure to India’s
diverse cultural traditions.

DIWALI CELEBRATION

Nyasa celebrated Diwali on October 20, 2025, with
vibrant enthusiasm and a strong sense of
community. The festivities began with a traditional
Lakshmi Pooja, followed by rangoli-making and
diya decoration activities that added colour and
warmth to the surroundings. As part of the
celebration, children were gifted gullaks to mark
the occasion. Extending the festive spirit beyond
the campus, the Nyasa team shared sweets with
security personnel, support staff, and nearby
village communities, fostering inclusivity and
togetherness.

MINI HEALTH CAMP

A medical health camp was organised on November

IIT Gandhinagar

16, 2025, in Basan, benefiting 111 community
members. Conducted under the “Social Change
Through Arts & Sports” initiative in collaboration
with IITGN’s Social Action and Policy Lab, the camp
focused on providing accessible primary healthcare.
In addition to routine check-ups, the initiative
promoted health awareness and strengthened
community engagement. The camp’s success was
supported by the voluntary efforts of Dr. Navdeep
Tiwari and the active participation of local residents.

EDUCATIONAL VISIT TO SCIENCE CITY

Nyasa students visited Science City on December
12, 2025, for an enriching educational experience.
The visit included explorations of the Nature Park,
Aquatic Gallery, and Astronomy and Space Science
Gallery. Students observed diverse ecosystems,
experienced marine life, and gained insights into
space science, including a live rocket demonstration.
The trip successfully fostered curiosity and made
science learning engaging and memorable.

WINTER CAMP

The Winter Camp, held on December 24, 2025,
engaged 80 students from Palaj, Basan, and
Prantiya government schools. The programme
began with a visit to the IITGN Central Library,
where students were introduced to academic
resources and independent learning techniques.
Through guided activities, they developed skills in
logical reasoning, time management, and financial
awareness. The second phase featured a character
design and animation workshop led by Mr
Himangshu Kothari, enabling students to create
and animate original characters, thereby integrating
creativity with digital learning.

SANJEEVANI 2026

Nyasa organised Sanjeevani 2026, its 10th annual
health camp, on January 11, 2026, in Giyod village.
The initiative provided free medical consultations
and essential medicines to over 480 community
members. Supported by a team of student
volunteers and nine medical professionals across
multiple specialisations, the camp continues to play
a significant role in improving access to healthcare
and strengthening community outreach.

VASANT PANCHAMI CELEBRATION

Nyasa students participated in the Vasant Panchami
celebrations on January 23, 2026, at IITGN. The
event included student-led prayers and a traditional
Saraswati Puja, fostering spiritual reflection and
cultural engagement. The celebration concluded

125

with the distribution of prasad, reinforcing a sense
of community and belonging.

HOLI CELEBRATION

Holi was celebrated on March 4, 2026, with great
enthusiasm, as Nyasa students joined the IITGN
community in day-long festivities. The celebration
emphasised togetherness and cultural unity, with
children actively participating in traditional
festivities. Each student was gifted a water gun,
adding to the joy of the occasion. The event served
as a platform for social integration and shared
cultural experiences.

CHETANA PROGRAMME

Chetana 8.0, Nyasa’s regular schooling initiative at
IIT Gandhinagar, provides a structured educational
framework for children of migrant workers and
contractual staff. The programme focuses on
strengthening foundational skills in Mathematics,
English, Hindi, Gujarati, and General Knowledge,
with the aim of facilitating integration into formal
schooling. Over the past year, nine students
successfully transitioned into regular schools. The
programme incorporates regular assessments,
critical thinking exercises, play-based learning, and
library sessions, ensuring holistic development
alongside academic growth.

AKANKSHA PROGRAMME

Akanksha 3.0 is a targeted mentorship initiative for
students from Basan and Palaj government schools,
aimed at preparing them for competitive
scholarship examinations such as JNVST, Gyansetu,
and NMMS. The programme focuses on mental
aptitude and arithmetic through structured
coaching, mentorship, and continuous performance
tracking. Supported by IITGN alumni contributions,
students receive study materials and specialised
resources. In the academic year 2025–26, eight
students successfully cleared the Gyansetu
scholarship
examination,
reflecting
the
programme’s impact in enabling educational
advancement.

rank in 2020—making it the most improved
institution in this category. Additionally, IITGN
secured the 2nd rank among the Top Three
Region-wise Engineering Colleges in Western
India. These achievements reflect the Institute’s
relentless pursuit of excellence in education and
research.

‘MAKER’S CURIOSITY’ PROGRAM

IITGN has developed and handed over an
indigenously designed Borewell Rescue System to
the NDRF, 06 Battalion, Vadodara, on Sept 23, 2025.
The winch-hoist-based system replaces manual rod
handling, ensuring safer and faster rescues.
Mentored by Prof Madhu Vadali, the staff-led
project was validated through multiple mock trials,
demonstrating IITGN’s commitment towards
societal impact.

IITGN HOSTS NATIONAL SYMPOSIUM

EDUCATION

IIT Gandhinagar organised a two-day symposium,

18–19, 2026, bringing together experts from IITGN,

centres. The visiting team included Kavita Desai
Shah, Co-Chairperson of the Business Women
Committee at GCCI.

IITGN ADVANCES SEMICONDUCTOR
TRAINING AND INNOVATION THROUGH
PROJECT SAMARTH

Work on Project SAMARTH (Silicon and Advanced
Semiconductor Manufacturing Research and
Training Hub) has commenced at IIT Gandhinagar.
A joint initiative of the Government of Gujarat,
MeitY, IITGN, and industry partners, the project
aims to build a skilled workforce for India’s

semiconductor sector. The centre will focus on
training, indigenous R&D, and the development of
advanced semiconductor devices and processes,
strengthening the country’s innovation ecosystem.
The initiative is expected to play a pivotal role in
supporting India’s semiconductor mission by
fostering
industry–academia
collaboration,
enabling hands-on training, and creating
opportunities for innovation and entrepreneurship
in this critical technology domain.
WELLBEING INITIATIVE EXPANDS STUDENT AND
CAMPUS SUPPORT SYSTEMS AT IITGN
The Wellbeing Initiative (WBI) at IIT Gandhinagar,
supported by the Indira Foundation, was launched
in January 2025 to strengthen mental and emotional
wellbeing across the campus community. During
Semester II, the initiative introduced a credit-based
course, “Emotion in Practice,” focused on emotional
awareness and wellness skills. Around 1,100
students participated through orientation sessions,
group discussions, and workshops on relationships,
sexual wellness, and emotional wellbeing in
collaboration with organisations including CHARMS
and CMHLP.
WBI also conducted training programmes for hostel
staff, faculty, and administrative staff to help
identify and support students in distress. A weekly
student listening circle, “Baithak,” continued to
provide a safe space for peer connection and
dialogue. In collaboration with CMHLP, IITGN also
launched a Student Guide Programme, training
undergraduate students to offer empathetic peer
support and referrals. Through these efforts and
campus-wide collaborations, IITGN is building a
sustainable and community-driven wellbeing
ecosystem.

WORKSHOP ON STUDENT WELLBEING

The Wellbeing Initiative at IITGN, supported by the
Indira Foundation, conducted a workshop on
student wellbeing for staff on Mar 18, 2026, in
collaboration with the Centre for Mental Health
Law and Policy. A follow-up to the Dec 2024 session,
the workshop focused on practical skills, identifying
student distress, active listening, and building
referral pathways for professional support—
further strengthening a responsive and supportive
campus environment.

CAMPUS
CONSTRUCTION UPDATES

M/s Mitimitra Consultants was appointed to provide architectural consultancy services for
the development of academic, administrative, and research facilities on campus. Construction
under Phase 2A, including faculty and staff housing, student hostels, and the Fire Engineering
Research Laboratory (FERL), is currently underway.

TRANSGENIC GREENHOUSE FACILITY

The construction of a new Transgenic Greenhouse facility has been successfully completed, and the Institute
has initiated the development of a clean room facility. The electrical infrastructure of the campus has also
been strengthened, with the installation of a 1000 kVA dry-type transformer at the Research Park, along
with an LT panel and an APFC panel.
In addition, a major upgrade at the main receiving substation was completed, including the installation of a
new HT panel and the creation of an additional ring to ensure a more reliable power supply throughout the
campus.

139

FIRE SAFETY TRAINING PROGRAMS

Fire safety training programs were conducted to enhance emergency preparedness. Horticulture and
Housekeeping staff (around 150 participants) attended sessions from 2 to 10 November 2025, security
personnel (approximately 70 participants) from 14 to 18 November 2025, and technical staff from different
laboratories (about 70 participants) on 6 February 2026. All sessions were conducted by a Safex-certified
trainer and included both theoretical instruction and hands-on demonstrations on the use of fire
extinguishers. Additionally, around 440 students from other institutes visited IIT Gandhinagar to explore its
facilities, including the STP, WTP, biogas plant, and sports complex.

GREEN CAMPUS

The Green Campus Committee successfully organised a Cycle Rally that was organised on Sunday, 29th
June 2025, from 6:00 AM to 8:30 AM as part of a joint initiative to promote environmental awareness within
the campus community. The rally encouraged participants to embrace cycling as a sustainable and healthy
mode of transport, reinforcing the Institute's commitment to a greener and more active campus lifestyle.
As part of the Swachhata Pakhwada initiative, the Green Campus Committee and Green Club organised a
series of events focused on cleanliness, waste management, and sustainability during October 2025. On
13th October 2025, the Green Campus Committee hosted an engaging Talk on Waste Segregation & E-Waste
Management from 5:00 PM to 6:00 PM. The session was delivered by Mr. Neeraj Shah, Environmental
Engineer at the Gujarat Pollution Control Board (GPCB), and invited the community to reflect on how the
waste discarded every day tells a story about our environment, our choices, and our collective future.
The Green Campus Committee successfully organised a Waste Collection and Cleanliness Drive on 11th
October 2025, from 8:00 AM to 11:00 AM. Teams comprising students and staff members were assigned
specific zones across the campus, including areas in the campus vicinity previously overlooked during
regular cleaning. Participants collected various types of waste such as plastic wrappers, bottles, metal
pieces, and other litter. The event fostered a strong sense of responsibility and teamwork among
participants, emphasising the importance of maintaining a clean and sustainable environment.
E-Waste Collection Bins were installed across the campus on 1st October 2025 to ensure better management
and disposal of electronic waste. In the hostel areas, one e-waste bin was placed on the ground floor of
each hostel, while bins were also installed in Block 5 and Block 6 in the academic area. The collected e-waste
is cleared every Saturday for proper and safe disposal, reinforcing the Institute's commitment to responsible
environmental stewardship.
As part of Swachhata Pakhwada, the Cleanest Hostel Award was organised from 8th to 15th October 2025.
A dedicated committee was formed to evaluate hostels based on cleanliness standards, and the results
were announced at the conclusion of the Pakhwada. The initiative encouraged healthy competition among
hostels and reinforced a culture of cleanliness and environmental responsibility across student residential
areas.
The Green Campus Committee and Green Club organised a series of creative and educational events during
the year to deepen environmental awareness across the campus community. A Dry Leaf Craft Workshop
and Competition invited participants to use dry leaves and natural discarded materials to create artwork,
decorative items, and utility products, with over 50 participants engaging in the workshop and submitting
competition entries showcasing innovative upcycling. A screening of the film “2012” attracted 60+
participants, followed by an informal discussion connecting the fictional narrative to real-world challenges
such as climate change, extreme weather events, and unsustainable practices.
An Essay Competition on “Beating the Plastic Pollution” received 15+ submissions from students across
various programmes, covering topics such as microplastic contamination, impacts on marine life, single-

140

Annual Report 2025-26 | www.iitgn.ac.in

use plastic alternatives, and circular economy models. The Green Club also collaborated with Nyasa School
to deliver a talk titled “Learning Sustainability the Fun Way,” introducing foundational sustainability concepts
to school-age children in an engaging and age-appropriate manner.
In collaboration with the Animal Welfare Club, the Green Club organised a Post-Uttarayan Thread and Kite
Waste Collection Drive on 17th January 2026 at 5:00 PM, assembling at the Basketball Court near Jaiswal
Mess. The initiative aimed to responsibly collect discarded kite threads and waste from public areas,
rooftops, and open spaces following the Uttarayan festival, reducing environmental hazards and promoting
compassion towards birds and wildlife whose lives are endangered by synthetic kite threads.
IIT Gandhinagar also hosted a distinguished guest visit from the Royal Government of Bhutan on February
12, 2026, as part of its ongoing engagement with sustainability and biodiversity. A presentation on “Campus
Biodiversity” was delivered by Prof. C. N. Pandey from 2:30 PM to 3:15 PM, reflecting the Institute’s
commitment to the holistic development of its community and its pride in nurturing a biodiverse and green
campus environment.

TINKERS LAB

The Tinkerers’ Lab (TL) remains the heartbeat of creativity at IIT Gandhinagar. We provide 24x7 access to a
playground of fabrication and electronics tools. TL is where students bridge the gap between classroom
theory and a functional prototype. This year, our arsenal of machines grew even stronger with the addition
of a Cobotic Arm. We are now capable of providing support to students interested in the realm of humanrobot interaction and collaboration. Our lab is fully equipped to tackle engineering challenges. A tinkerer
has access to a range of equipment, from 3D printers and laser cutters to oscilloscopes and PCB milling
machines.

A CULTURE OF CONSTANT TINKERING

With an average daily footfall of 16–20 students, the lab is always filled with clink-clanks. It serves as a hub
for coursework, DIY hobbies, and startup prototyping. This year, we hosted workshops on Laser Cutting,
3D Printing, and operation of the new Cobotic Arm, ensuring students have the technical foundation and
confidence to make the most of the available gear.
Beyond individual projects, TL also continues to be the backbone for major competitive teams; we provided
the space, materials, and equipment necessary for Baja SAE and DD Robocon to design and refine their
builds for the national stage. To keep this momentum going, we are currently gearing up to host Buildathon,
a rapid design and manufacturing competition that will challenge students to turn concepts into physical
models under deadlines.

FUTURE GOALS: LOWERING THE BARRIER

As a student-managed hub, our goal is to make "tinkering" as intuitive as possible for everyone on campus.
Our upcoming initiatives include:
● Learning Tools: We are hoping to develop demo videos and infographics for our machinery to make
the equipment less intimidating for beginners and ensure safe, independent use.
● Project Repository: We plan to build a comprehensive digital archive of projects supported by TL. This
is a Herculean task which we hope is continued by our future batches! By documenting everything from
small weekend repairs to complex startup prototypes, we hope to create a legacy of innovation. TL
remains an essential part of the IIT Gandhinagar ecosystem. TL is a space where students don’t just
learn engineering, they interact and engage with it until they finally become immersed in it!

the digital ecosystem of IIT Gandhinagar. With a focus on reliability, scalability, security, and user‑centric
services, ISTF provides seamless information systems, networking, cybersecurity, and computational
support to the campus community. The year 2025–26 witnessed major advancements in infrastructure

modernization,
in‑house
technological
development, administrative process automation.
ISTF is responsible for a wide range of critical
technology operations, including:
● Management of servers, data centers, networks,
and security systems (firewalls, CCTV, access
control)
● Administration of email services, internet
connectivity, and IT-enabled classrooms
● Operation and maintenance of HPC facilities
and computer labs
● Lifecycle management of ERP, hardware,
software, and enterprise security systems
● Through efficient management of these
essential services, ISTF ensures 24×7 operational
continuity and a secure, robust IT environment.

INFRASTRUCTURE DEVELOPMENT &
IN‑HOUSE TECHNOLOGICAL
ADVANCEMENTS

● Establishment of Computer Labs: Four
modern
computer
laboratories
were
established, equipped with up‑to‑date hardware
and licensed software, improving academic and
research computing facilities.
● Upgradation of eOffice Modules: All eOffice
modules were upgraded to the latest versions,
improving security, performance, and user
experience with several new features and bug
fixes.
● Single Sign-On (SSO) Enhancements: SSO was
expanded across multiple institutional systems,
enabling unified login and improving access
control, efficiency, and user convenience.

KEY ACHIEVEMENTS

On‑Premises
Email
Infrastructure
Modernisation:
The
on‑premises
email
infrastructure was modernised using secure,
scalable open‑source technologies and integrated
with SSO for centralized authentication. Automated
user
provisioning
streamlined
account
management, while advanced spam and threat
filtering improved email security. Mailbox quota
monitoring and storage‑utilisation tools were
added, along with an enhanced webmail interface
for a clearer and more user‑friendly experience.
Virtualisation
&
Backup
Infrastructure
Strengthening: Virtual machines were provisioned
and continuously monitored to support critical
academic and administrative services, backed by
structured server and VM backup mechanisms.

Routine backup verification processes were
implemented to ensure disaster‑readiness, data
integrity, and uninterrupted service availability.
HPC & Infrastructure Optimisation Using Legacy
Hardware: The team designed and deployed a
hybrid High‑Performance Computing (HPC) and
virtualisation environment by reusing available
legacy hardware, integrating modern operating
systems and open‑source technologies to extend
hardware life and significantly reduce capital
expenditure. This optimized environment now
efficiently supports research computation, virtual
machines, and various administrative workloads,
ensuring improved utilisation of existing
institutional resources.
Next-Generation Firewall Deployment: A
Next‑Generation Firewall (NGFW), greatly improving
network security, visibility, and threat detection.
The institute’s internet capacity was upgraded to a
10 Gbps link, enhancing speed and reliability for all
users. Campus‑wide planning for Wi‑Fi 6 was
initiated to improve wireless performance and
scalability. Additionally, a CERT‑In compliant
honeypot server was deployed to proactively detect
and analyse cyber threats targeting the network.
CCTV & Access Control Enhancements: CCTV at
the Research Park was migrated to Genetec VMS
for centralized monitoring. Servers were virtualized
for improved availability, the Access Control Server
upgraded to Windows Server 2022, and surveillance
expanded with 25 new cameras under Phase-II
(Jeet Royal Block B & C).

IMS FORMS AND PROCESS
REENGINEERING:

● Payout Module: Automated payroll, improving
accuracy and transparency
● Integrated Leave Module: Unified leave
system integrated with eOffice workflows
● Telephone Reimbursement & CEA: eOfficebased approvals with IMS integration for
tracking
● Project Registration: Single consolidated form
with end-to-end eOffice processing
● Faculty Recruitment Portal: Enhanced with
automated publication data, scoring, and
reduced manual effort

IIT Gandhinagar

MEDICAL CENTRE

During the year, the Medical Centre at IIT
Gandhinagar further strengthened its healthcare
services through enhancements in clinical
expertise, infrastructure, digitalization, and
community health initiatives. The Centre expanded
its specialist care by onboarding three consultant
specialists: Dr Aakriti Jha (Psychiatry), Dr Jainisha
Patel (Gynaecologist), and Dr Maulik Kapadia
(Paediatrics). In addition, Ms Joshi Nidhiben joined
the Medical Centre as an Assistant Staff Nurse,
further augmenting the Centre's clinical and patient
care capabilities.
The Medical Centre renovation has started during
the year to enhance the quality and efficiency of
healthcare services. A dedicated, fully equipped
space was allocated for Physiotherapy services,
enabling the delivery of more comprehensive
rehabilitation
services.
Additional
medical
equipment, including wheelchairs, examination
lights and other patient-care resources, were
procured to strengthen clinical infrastructure and
improve the
overall quality of patient care.
To improve access to essential medicines, an Apollo
Pharmacy was established on the IIT Gandhinagar
campus. The pharmacy operates throughout the
year, 365 days a year, from 8:00 a.m. to 10:00 p.m.,
ensuring convenient availability of pharmaceutical
services for the campus community. A cashless
medicine facility has also been introduced for
eligible beneficiaries, including (i) IIT Gandhinagar
students; (ii) regular IIT Gandhinagar employees
and their spouses and dependent children; and (iii)
contractual employees who are entitled to free
consultation and medicines in accordance with the
provisions of their appointment letters.
The Centre conducted a TB Awareness Programme,
an Influenza Vaccination Drive, monthly Mamta
Divas immunization sessions for children in
collaboration with PHC Palaj, and annual student
health check-ups and regular staff health check-up
upon joining time. Several Medical Centre
operations were digitized, improving service
efficiency and record management.

DAY CARE CENTRE

The IIT Gandhinagar Day Care Centre, established
in March 2014, is a community-driven initiative
designed to provide a safe, nurturing, and
supportive environment for the children of IITGN

143

families. Located within the residential quarters,
the centre emphasises the comfort, well-being, and
holistic development of every child, creating a
warm and homely atmosphere. Supported by a
dedicated team, the daycare follows a thoughtfully
designed curriculum tailored to individual needs
and interests, ensuring meaningful engagement
and strong caregiver–child relationships.
The centre is distinguished by its innovative
developmental programmes that integrate music,
dance, play, and exploration as essential tools for
learning and growth.
Key initiatives include:
● Morning Programme: Introduced in July 2018,
this
non-traditional
programme
blends
structured learning with creative exploration.
Activities such as library visits and bird-watching
sessions broaden children’s experiences while
fostering curiosity and early learning.
● Kids Supported Agriculture (KSA): This handson gardening initiative engages children in
cultivating fruits and vegetables, encouraging
sensory exploration and a deeper connection
with nature. The excitement of harvesting
produce such as methi, spinach, coriander, and
brinjal, and using them in meals, enhances their
learning experience.
● Cooking Sessions: Through a no-flame cooking
programme, children participate in fun culinary
activities such as making sandwiches, laddoos,
bhel, and strawberry yogurt. These sessions
promote creativity while introducing basic
cooking skills in a safe environment.
● Sports Activities: The centre conducts inclusive,
non-traditional sports activities focused on
open-ended play, ensuring that every child,
regardless of ability, can participate, build
confidence, and develop physically.
● Other Events: A range of celebrations, including
pyjama parties and festivals such as Diwali,
Christmas, and Navratri, foster social interaction
and cultural awareness. Daily routines are
further enriched through music, art, yoga,
storytelling, and sensory play, supporting the
development of creativity, concentration, and
motor skills.
Looking ahead, the Day Care Centre remains
committed to providing high-quality care and a
nurturing environment, ensuring that every child
continues to thrive and grow holistically.

SCHOLARSHIPS
SHAH SCHOLARSHIP

Aarushi Shah (MSc 2019) established the Shah
Scholarship in honour of her mother, Dr Avani
Shah, and grandmother, Late Dr Pushpa Shah,
whose inspiring journeys as doctors and trailblazers
deeply shaped her path. Aarushi graduated from
IITGN in 2019 with an MSc in Cognitive Science. She
has worked at the University of Cambridge, UK, at
the Winton Centre for Risk and Evidence
Communication, and is currently associated with
Noora Health. Her contribution will support female
MSc students for international and domestic
internships.

P P SUKUMARAN SCHOLARSHIP

Jithin Prabha (BTech/ME 2016) established the P P
Sukumaran Scholarship at IITGN in memory of his
late father, Shri P P Sukumaran, who embodied
perseverance and dedication to education. Jithin,
currently a Mechanical Engineer at Lam Research,
San Francisco, is an IITGN alumnus and holds a
master’s degree from Purdue University. This
scholarship will support and inspire future

generations of students, continuing
tradition of alumni giving back.

IITGN’s

LILA WATI NAYYAR SCHOLARSHIP

Mr Mohinder L Nayyar has instituted the Lila Wati
Nayyar Scholarship. The scholarship of Rs 1 lakh
per year will support undergraduate students. Mr
Nayyar is an internationally recognised expert in
piping, valves, materials, and engineering codes
and standards. He has been honoured by the
American Society of Mechanical Engineers (ASME),
including the Melvin R Green Medal.

NEW CLASSROOMS NAMED IN
HONOUR OF DR JAGDISH PATEL AND
MRS HARSHA PATEL

Dr Jagdish Patel and Mrs Harsha Patel of the Jibaben
Patel Foundation have made a second generous
contribution to IITGN. In recognition of this, two
classrooms in Academic Block 10 have been named
the Jagdish Patel Learning Theatre and the Harsha
Patel Learning Theatre.

COLLABORATION WITH ENPHASE
SOLAR ENERGY PVT LTD

IITGN entered a Memorandum of Understanding
(MoU) on October 28, 2025, with Enphase Solar
Energy Pvt Ltd to collaborate on a Corporate Social
Responsibility (CSR) project titled “Smart ESD
(Energy Storage Device) Safety Diagnostic System.”
The collaboration is part of the broader initiative
“Empowering Research in Indigenous Development
of EV Sub-systems (E-RIDES)” and aims to advance
research in energy storage safety diagnostics.

in

degree

in

summer programme where students across India
work in teams to prototype, pitch, and patent

at IIT Gandhinagar, including scholarships for

programmes,
grassroots

children of construction workers on campus.
Jankidevi Bajaj Gram Vikas Sanstha has
contributed CSR funds towards the Rupa Rahul
Bajaj Scholarship for Women in Engineering. IITGN
has received a generous CSR contribution from
Radix Electrosystems Pvt Ltd towards the
Competitive Mindset Initiative.
