PEOPLE
DISTINGUISHED
HONORARY PROFESSOR

DR A S KIRAN KUMAR

Dr A S Kiran Kumar is an Indian space
scientist and former chairman of the Indian
Space Research Organisation. Dr Kiran
Kumar obtained an honours degree in
physics from National College, Bangalore in
1971, an MSc degree (electronics) from
Bangalore University in 1973 and an MTech
in physical engineering with distinction
from Indian Institute of Science, Bangalore
in 1975. In recognition of his contributions,
he was conferred the Padma Shri award by
the President of India in 2014. He has
contributed to the design and development
of more than 50 electro-optical imaging
sensors flown on space-borne platforms
starting from Bhaskara TV payload in 1979
to the payloads onboard the Mars Orbiter
Mission in 2013.

PROF NITISH THAKOR

Prof Nitish Thakor is a professor of
biomedical engineering, electrical and
computer engineering, and neurology at
Johns Hopkins University, and leads the
Laboratory for Neuroengineering. He is also
the director of the Singapore Institute for
Neurotechnology at the National University
of
Singapore.
He
obtained
his
undergraduate degree from IIT Bombay in
1974 and a PhD from the University of
Wisconsin, Madison in 1981. Prof Thakor is

the recipient of the Centennial Medal from
the School of Engineering, University of
Wisconsin (2008), Honorary Membership
from Alpha Eta Mu Beta Biomedical
Engineering student Honor Society. He
received the award of Technical Excellence
in Neuroengineering from IEEE Engineering
in Medicine and Biology Society and the
Distinguished Alumnus Award in 2012 from
IIT Bombay and the Centennial Medal from
the University of Wisconsin, Madison School
of Engineering in 2012.

PROF SHEKHAR C MANDE

Prof Shekhar Mande is an honorary
distinguished faculty of National Centre for
Cell Science Complex and former director
general of the Council of Scientific and
Industrial Research (CSIR). Prof Mande
received his MSc in physics from the
University of Nagpur in 1984 and PhD in
molecular biophysics, from IISc Bangalore
in 1991. Following his PhD, he worked as a
postdoctoral researcher at Rijksuniversiteit
Groningen in the Netherlands. He is
currently distinguished professor at the
Bioinformatics Centre, Savitribai Phule
Pune University, the position which is
generously funded by Dr Anand Deshpande.
He is an elected fellow of all three science
academies namely, the National Science
Academy of India, Allahabad; the Indian

Academy of Sciences, Bangalore and the
Indian National Science Academy, New
Delhi. He has been recognised by many
awards, the most notable one being the
Shanti Swarup Bhatnagar Prize for biological
sciences in 2005. He served as the director
general of CSIR and secretary of DSIR, Govt
of India during Oct 2018 - April 2022.

PROF SURENDRA PRASAD

Prof Surendra Prasad served IIT Delhi for
over four decades in several academic and
administrative capacities including the post
of the director. He received the Vikram
Sarabhai Research Award in electronics and
telecommunications (1987), the Shanti
Swarup Bhatnagar Prize for engineering
sciences (1988), the Om Prakash Bhasin
Prize for research in electronics and
communications (1994), the VASVIK Award
for information technology (2006), the
Lifetime Achievement Award of the Systems
Society of India (2011), the Distinguished
Alumnus Award of IIT Kharagpur. He was
also honored with an honorary doctorate by
Loughborough University, UK in 2007. He is
a fellow of the Indian National Academy of
Engineering, the Indian National Science
Academy, the Indian Academy of Sciences
and the National Academy of Science and
has been a member of the governing body
of CSIR and CSIR Society, Government of

152

Annual Report 2025-26 | www.iitgn.ac.in

India and boards of many IITs, NITs and
other engineering institutes.

DR KESHAV DATTATREYA NAYAK

Keshav Dattatreya Nayak is a distinguished
scientist at the Defence Research and
Development Organisation (DRDO) who
served as the director general for Micro
Electronic
Devices
&
Computational
Systems (MED & CoS). He completed his BE
(Electronics) in 1974 from the Visvesvaraya
College of Engineering, Bangalore University
with distinction, and PhD in 1981 in
microelectronics from IIT Bombay. Dr Nayak
has received several awards for his scientific
contributions such as the DRDO Award for
his contribution to Integrated Guided
Missile Development Program in 1989, the
IETE--IRSI (83) Award for 1997-98 for
outstanding contribution in the field of
radar and microwave systems, and the
Technology Day Invention Award by
National
Research
Development
Corporation.

PROF SHAILESH NAYAK

Dr Shailesh Nayak is currently the director
at the National Institute of Advanced
Studies, Indian Institute of Science Campus,
Bangalore. He was distinguished scientist
during 2015-2018 and secretary at the
Ministry of Earth Sciences, Government of
India, during August 2008-2015, and
provided leadership for programmes
related to earth system sciences. He
obtained a PhD degree in geology from the
MS University of Baroda in 1980. He has
been credited with launching many research
programmes related to monsoon, air-sea
interaction,
changing
water
cycle,
atmospheric
chemistry,
coastal
vulnerability, climate services, etc. with the
US, UK, Germany, Japan, Australia, Norway,
and South Korea. He pioneered the
development
of
algorithms
and
methodologies for the application of
remote sensing to the coastal and marine
environment and generated a baseline
database of the Indian coast, and developed
services for fishery and ocean state
forecasts. He was conferred the prestigious
ISC Vikram Sarabhai Memorial Award 2012
as well as Bhaskara Award for 2009,
Fellowship of the International Society of
Photogrammetry & Remote Sensing (ISPRS)
and elected member of the International
Academy of Astronautics (IAA) for his
outstanding contributions in remote
sensing and GIS.

SCHOLAR-INRESIDENCE
DR PAOLO BIAGI

Dr Paolo Biagi did his Master (Italian Dott)
from Facoltà di Lettere e Filosofia, Università
degli Studi di Milano, Italy and PhD from
London University Institute of Archaeology,
UK. He is currently working as a professor of
prehistory and protohistory in the Ca'
Foscari University of Venice, Italy. His
research
interests
include
history,
anthropology, archaeology, art history and
geography. He is a renowned expert of
Harappan studies.

DR RANDALL LAW

Dr Randall Law is an expert in Harappan
archaeology and has pioneered the studies
of raw material provenance during the
Harappan period (c 2600-1900 BCE)
primarily for the site of Harappa, district
Sahiwal, Pakistan. His doctoral thesis on
“Inter-Regional Interaction and Urbanism in
the Ancient Indus Valley: A Geologic
Provenance Study of Harappa’s Rock and
Mineral Assemblage” is a pioneering effort
in bringing together all the raw material
sources spanning over a millennium and
better understand the urbanism at
Harappa.

DR DENNYS FRENEZ

Dr Frenez did his MA (Honours) in
palaeoethnology and PhD in archaeology
from the Department of Archaeology,
University of Bologna, Italy. His research
interests include bronze age state-level
urban societies in Asia with specific
reference to the Indus (Pakistan & NW India)
and Magan civilisations (SE Arabia), iron age
semi-nomadic cultures in SW Arabia
(Dhofar, Oman) and their interaction with
ancient South Arabian kingdoms and
international trade, long-range trade
exchanges and cross-cultural interactions,
ancient
production
technique
and
technologies,
ancient
administrative
(including
writing)
technologies
and
procedures and archaeometry applied to
ancient
production
techniques
and
technologies.

PROF BRIAN BROPHY

Prof Brian Brophy did his BS from Montana
State University, MA from California State
University, Los Angeles, and MFA from U C
Riverside. He is the director of Theater Arts
at Caltech. He also serves as the artistic
director of MACH 33: The Festival of New
Science-Driven Plays. He directed Tales of
the Lost Formicans; The Laramie Project;
The Waiting Room; Book of Days. Prior to
Caltech, he taught at UC Riverside, Pomona
College, Banaras Hindu University, and
CSULA. He has taught play analysis,
contemporary theater; history of theater;
acting
styles,
intermediate
acting;
introduction to acting, theater for social
change and movement for actors. His
research interests include: theatre for social
change in India, international collaboration
and partnership in the transformation of
culture through dialogue, storytelling and
friendship.

PROF MARIA JOÃO AMANTE

Dr Maria João Amante, PhD is the director of
Portuguese Parliament Library, Portugal
since July 2022. From Sept 2020 to July 2022,
she served as the director of Braga Public
Library, Portugal. From 2004 to 2019, she
served as the library director at ISCTEInstituto Universitário de Lisboa, Portugal.
Since 2017, she has also been collaborating
with the IITGN Writing Studio and IITGN
Library. She is a member of several
professional associations and author of
several articles and book chapters on topics
such as information literacy, research
information management, citizen science,
open access, faculty/librarian relationships,
competences development, and scientific
writing. She has participated in several
scientific committees too.

DR KASTHURI VENKATESWARAN

Dr Kasthuri Venkateswaran received a PhD
in marine microbiology from Annamalai
University, India in 1981 and was conferred
a doctorate in agriculture from Hiroshima
University, Japan in 1990. At present, Dr
Venkat is a senior research scientist at NASA
– Jet Propulsion Laboratory and supports
Biotechnology and Planetary Protection
Group. He has spent over 40 years in
research in the field of marine, food, and
environmental microbiology. He has
applied his research in molecular microbial
analysis to better understand the ecological
aspects of microorganisms.

DR SATHYANARAYANAN
MUNDAYOOR

Dr Sathyanarayanan Mundayoor did his BSc
and MA in linguistics from Bombay
University, post-MA diploma in adult &
continuing education from Delhi University,
postgraduate diploma in journalism from
Bhavans, Mumbai. He is the coordinator at
Lohit Youth Library Network, Tezu,
Arunachal Pradesh. His research interests
include school education, reading skills,
non-formal education in rural and tribal
communities, and the production of reading
material
in
marginalised
North-East
languages,
history
of
aviation
in
independent India with respect to NorthEast regions.

MR SUMAN S CHOUDHURY

(Artist-In-Residence)
Mr Suman Choudhury did his Bachelor of
Fine Arts from Kala Bhavana, Visva Bharati.
He is a visiting faculty at the National
Institute of Design (NID) Vijayawada, NID
Gandhinagar and NID Kurukshetra. He is
also a collaborator at the Cardiological
Society of India (CSI) in their projects to
educate
people
and
create
social
consciousness about coronary diseases.

MR DON CHACKO PALATHARA

(Artist-In-Residence)
Mr Don Chacko Palathara obtained a BSc in
physics from St Berchman’s College,
Mahatma Gandhi University, Kottayam,
master’s degree in information technology
from University of Tasmania, Hobart and
Diploma in Direction, Screenwriting and
Production from International Film School,
Sydney. Mr Palathara is a film director,
screenwriter, and documentary filmmaker

IIT Gandhinagar

from Kerala. Known for working on small
budgets, his films are expositions of local
Kerala culture and study of human nature.
Palathara's films have gained accolades at
several international film festivals, including
Moscow
International
Film
Festival,
International Film Festival Rotterdam and
International Film Festival of Kerala.

MR ARGHA MANNA

(Artist-In-Residence)
Mr Argha Manna did his BSc and MSc from
the University of Calcutta. His research
interest includes bringing the history of
science into life through graphic narratives
including comics, data comics, illustrated
essays, infographics, and painting. Before
joining IIT Gandhinagar he was working as a
journalist in Anandabazar Patrika / The
Telegraph.

GUEST
PROFESSORS
PROF BIJOY H BORUAH

Prof Boruah completed his PhD from the
University of Guelph (Canada) in 1984. He
has taught philosophy in various institutions
like IIT Delhi, IIT Ropar, and IIT Jammu. Prof
Boruah’s
research
interests
include
philosophy
of
mind,
philosophical
aesthetics, history of modern western
philosophy, ethics and value theory,
metaphysics of the self, technoscience and
transhumanism, metaphysics of virtual
reality, artificial intelligence and human
agency, among others.

PROF P AJITHPRASAD

Prof Ajithprasad, a professor in the
department of archaeology and ancient
history, the Maharaja Sayajirao University
(MSU), Baroda. He did his BSc from the
University of Calicut, MA from the MSU
Baroda, PG Diploma from the School of
Archaeology, Archaeological Survey of India,
and PhD from the MSU Baroda. Prof
Ajithprasad has been associated with the
MSU Baroda since 1990. His research is
focused on prehistoric archaeology,
quaternary environmental adaptations and
Harappan studies. He possesses the life
membership of the Indian Society for
Prehistoric and Quaternary Academic
bodies and the Indian Archaeological
Society, and is also a member of the
Geological Society of India.

DR DARSHAN PANDYA

Dr Darshan V Pandya is a philosopher,
author, and life guru. He holds a PhD, MSc,
and BSc(Eng) (Hons), with advanced studies
in chemical engineering and operational
research at the Imperial College, London.
With over four decades of experience in IT,
academia, and operational research, Dr
Pandya has held key roles across the globe,
including lecturer at the University of
Singapore and operational research analyst
at BP Chemicals in the UK. His technical
career includes work with leading
companies such as Perot Systems and
eFunds. Since retiring in 2014, Dr Pandya
has become the founder of 'Svadharma Your Own Righteousness,' a philosophy
promoting spirituality and morality in the

twenty first century. In 2017, he published
his book, ‘Svadharma - Your Own
Righteousness’, on Amazon. He remains
actively engaged in community activities
and global issues, with a passion for
philosophy, travel, and music.

PROF S K TANDON

Prof Sampat Kumar Tandon is an Indian
geologist and a professor emeritus of
geology at the University of Delhi. He is a
former pro-vice chancellor of Delhi
University, Sir J C Bose Chair Professor of
the Department of Earth and Environmental
Sciences at the Indian Institute of Science
Education and Research (IISER), Bhopal and
a D N Wadia Chair Professor of the
Department of Earth Sciences at the Indian
Institute of Technology, Kanpur. Prof
Tandon is known for his geological studies
of the Siwalik rocks and is an elected fellow
of all the three major Indian science
academies namely the Indian Academy of
Sciences, Indian National Science Academy,
and the National Academy of Sciences, India
as well as The World Academy of Sciences.
The Council of Scientific and Industrial
Research, the apex agency of the
Government of India for scientific research,
awarded him the Shanti Swarup Bhatnagar
Prize for Science and Technology, one of the
highest Indian science awards for his
contributions to earth, atmosphere, ocean
and planetary sciences in 1988.

PROF DEBI P SARKAR

Prof Sarkar is an Indian biochemist,
immunologist, and virologist. He worked as
a visiting professor of chemistry, Ashoka
University, Sonipat, Haryana, and was the
head of the Department of Biochemistry
and dean FIAS at the University of Delhi
South Campus. He also served as the
director of IISER Mohali from 2017 to 2019.
On the scientific front, he is renowned for
his research on Sendai virus and developing
reconstituted viral envelopes. An elected
fellow of the Indian Academy of Sciences,
National Academy of Sciences, and the
Indian National Science Academy, Prof
Sarkar was awarded the Shanti Swarup
Bhatnagar Prize for Science and Technology
in 1988 for his contributions to biological
sciences.

PROF GANGA KV PRAKHYA

Prof Prakhya completed his Phd from
Cambridge University, UK, in 1989. He has
served as the head of design, chief design
engineer, and graduate engineer, in
organisations such as Sir Robert McAlpine
Ltd and Esquire Engineers & Consultants.
Prof Prakhya has authored 21 papers in
professional journals, has two book
chapters to his credits, and won ten
academic scholarships/prizes, gold medal/
awards, including one of India's top
scholarships for fully funded study at
Cambridge.

PROF RAVINDRA PRATAP SINGH

Dr Ravindra Pratap Singh is a senior
professor and head of the Division at the
Atomic, Molecular, and Optical Physics
Division at Physical Research Laboratory,
Ahmedabad. He also holds positions as a
visiting professor at IIT Roorkee, adjunct

153

professor at IIT Mandi, and SRM University
AP, Amaravati. His primary research areas
include light scattering, structured light
beams, nonlinear optics, quantum optics,
and quantum information. Dr Singh has
played a pivotal role in advancing quantum
science and technology, including the
development of experimental programs at
PRL. His groundbreaking work on quantum
key distribution (QKD) and free-space
quantum communication earned him
recognition as a Fellow of NASI. He has
contributed significantly to various national
and international projects on quantum
technologies, including collaborations with
institutions like C-DOT, ISRO, and Tel Aviv
University. A prolific academician, Dr Singh
has supervised over 15 Ph D students and
published more than 100 peer-reviewed
papers.

PROF JACKRIT SUTHAKORN

Dr Jackrit Suthakorn earned his bachelor's
degree in mechanical engineering from
Mahidol University in 1995. That same year,
he joined the university's department of
mechanical engineering as a faculty
member and received a full scholarship
from the Royal Thai Government to pursue
advanced studies in the U S. He earned a
master's degree in control engineering from
Michigan Technological University in 1998
and a PhD in robotics from Johns Hopkins
University in 2003. While studying, he
worked with GE Medical Systems in
biomedical engineering from 2001 to 2002.
He returned to Mahidol University in 2003,
where he became the vice-department
chair (2004-2007) and established the
Center for Biomedical and Robotics
Technology (BART LAB) in 2004. In 2006, he
co-founded Thailand's first biomedical
engineering department, where he has
served as chair since its inception. He has
led various research projects and has
earned numerous awards, including
Mahidol University’s Outstanding Academic
Person Award in 2011.

DR RAJIV SINHA

Dr Sinha completed his PhD in fluvial
geomorphology and sedimentology from
the University of Cambridge, UK. He is
currently a professor in the Department of
Earth Sciences, IIT Kanpur. Some of his
research interests include earth surface
dynamics: terrain characterisation and
landscape analysis using Earth observation
data, soil moisture and crop water stress
estimation using drone-based thermal
imaging, wetland hydrology, connectivity
and dynamics, river science: river dynamics,
flood risk assessment, sediment dynamics,
river response to climate change, rivers and
ancient civilization, groundwater structure
and dynamics: historical time series
analysis, aquifer stratigraphy, among
others.

SHRI JAG MOHAN LAL

Shri Jag Mohan Lal, a distinguished civil
engineer, holds a BSc and BE in Civil
Engineering with Honours from the
University of Roorkee (now IIT Roorkee). He
also holds a PG Diploma in International
Trade Law and Alternative Dispute
Resolution from the Indian Law Institute,

154

Annual Report 2025-26 | www.iitgn.ac.in

Delhi, and completed a three months
course in Urban Development Management
from Birmingham, UK (1989). After a brief
tenure with UP PWD, he joined CPWD as a
direct recruit in 1965, eventually retiring as
Additional Director General in 2002. During
his career, Shri Lal led prestigious projects
like Vigyan Bhavan Annexe, the Institute of
Higher Tibetan Studies, and the Parliament
Library Project, where he developed a Total
Quality Management System, earning the
first ISO 9002 certification for a construction
project. He also managed the maintenance
of VVIP buildings and civil airports across
India. Currently, Shri Lal serves as an
arbitrator and Chairman/Member of
Dispute Review Boards for World Bank/
NHAI projects, having issued arbitration
awards in 119 cases. He is also a visiting
faculty member at several esteemed
institutions.

PROF KENNETH E GONSALVES

Dr Kenneth E Gonsalves holds a PhD from
the University of Massachusetts at Amherst
(1984) and has completed postdoctoral
work at MIT. He has served as an assistant
professor in Stevens Institute of Technology,
University of Connecticut, and as a visiting
professor in Harvard University, among
others. His expertise spans materials
synthesis, organic polymer chemistry,
organometallic
polymers,
and
nanostructured materials. He has served as
associate director for S&T Americas at the
Office of Naval Research Global and worked
as a scientific advisor in ININ Mexico. A
member of the American Chemical Society
and Materials Research Society, Dr
Gonsalves’ research focuses on advanced
materials and nanofabrication techniques.

SHRI SAMIR PATEL

Shri Patel is the chief strategy officer (CSO)
at StarIC. Throughout his extensive career
spanning more than 35 years, he has played
integral roles in growing start-ups into large
international
corporations,
including
Sankalp Semiconductor where he grew it
into a more than 1000-person company,

Rambus where he as an early team member
grew its international operations, and
Catalyst Semiconductor where he was a key
designer. Prior to StarIC, Shri Patel was the
chief executive officer of Blue Cheetah
Analog Design which focused on rapid
generators of die-to-die and chiplet
interconnect IP. He has also worked with
other companies, including National
Semiconductor and Sun Microsystems. He
is an alumnus of Stanford University, UC
Santa Barbara, and IIT Bombay.

PROF NUNO GUIMARAES

Prof Guimarães is a full professor (Professor
Catedrático) at ISCTE IUL. He graduated in
electrotechnical
engineering
at
the
Technical University of Lisbon, Instituto
Superior Técnico, Portugal, in 1983, where
he also completed his MSc (1987) and PhD
(1992). He received the title of Agregado em
Informática in July 1999, from the University
of Lisbon. From 1986 to 1997, he taught at
the
electrotechnical
and
computer
engineering department of IST/UTL, and
from 1997 to 2012 at the informatics
department of the Faculty of Sciences,
University of Lisbon.

DR DEBANIK ROY

Dr Debanik Roy is a senior scientist (SO-H)
and adjunct professor in the Division of
Remote Handling & Robotics at Bhabha
Atomic Research Centre (BARC), Mumbai,
and the Homi Bhabha National Institute,
Department of Atomic Energy, Government
of India. He completed his PhD from
Jadavpur University, Kolkata, and his
research spans various fields of robotics
such as: robot grippers, gripper sensors,
web-mediated
robotics,
flexible
manipulator, bio-inspired robotics, telemanipulation, etc.

PROF ABHIJIT PANDYA

Prof Abhijit Pandya is a seasoned expert in
computer science and engineering, with
focus on artificial intelligence (AI) in
developing decision support systems for
medicine. With 37 years of experience in AI,

machine
learning,
and
biomedical
engineering, he has collaborated across
disciplines and worked on research leading
to patents, clinical trials, and stem cell
therapies. He has supervised ten PhD
dissertations and 40 master’s theses, with
over 200 peer-reviewed publications. Prof
Pandya’s extensive industry involvement
includes
multimillion-dollar
funded
research. Despite a rare disease diagnosis
in the 1990s, he remains dedicated to
advancing clinical research and AI-driven
healthcare innovations.

PROF NITIN KUMAR TRIPATHI

Prof Tripathi completed his PhD from the
Indian Institute of Technology, Kanpur in
1994. His research interests include remote
sensing and GIS for environment, coastal,
agriculture, disaster and health, internet
GIS, and machine learning applications in
GIS, among others. He was awarded the
DAE Young Scientist Award in 1994 and the
AICTE Career Award for Young Teacher in
1996.

PROF SUMIT ROY

Prof Roy completed his PhD from the
University of California, Santa Barbara in
1988. He served as a professor at the
University of Washington, Seattle and was
the program lead of Innovate Beyond 5G,
5G-to-xG Initiative, US DoD (OUSD R&E). His
research interests include Beyond 5G
(mobile cellular, satellite and WiFi), Internet
of Things, and Autonomous and SoftwareDefined Networks.

SHRI SUBODH KUMAR JAISWAL

Shri Jaiswal served as the director of the
Central Bureau of Investigation, and head,
National Central Bureau of INTERPOL, India
from May 2021 - May 2023. His research
interests
include
human
resource
management,
strategic
management,
training,
project
management,
risk
assessment, change management, and
organisation communication (external and
internal) among others.

ALUMNI
RELATIONS
IITGN alumni remain closely connected with the Institute through a range of initiatives that
promote continued engagement and a strong sense of community. Over the years, alumni
participation has grown significantly, fostering meaningful interactions, stronger networking
opportunities, and avenues for mentorship and career guidance. Alumni regularly contribute by supporting
student-led activities, participating in reunions and institute events, and visiting the campus. Through these
engagements, they continue to strengthen their relationship with the Institute while inspiring and guiding
the current student community.

ANNUAL ALUMNI GIVING

In the financial year 2025–2026, 9.7% of IITGN alumni contributed a total of Rs. 28 lakhs towards various
institutional initiatives. Out of 4,746 alumni, 461 extended their support through financial contributions.
These generous donations supported a wide range of causes, including the Gratitude Gift for support staff,
international internship opportunities, student support and scholarships, Motorsport, social outreach
programs, housekeeping and workers' welfare, sports and cultural events, and entrepreneurship
development.

ALUMNI SUPPORT IN THE FINANCIAL YEAR 2025-2026

●
●
●
●
●

Total alumni: 4746
Alumni donors: 461 / 9.7%
Alumni donation: Rs 28 lakh
14.8 % / 99 recently graduated students of 2025 donated Rs. 58 thousand toward the batch gift
69.4 % / 3297 Alumni donated at least once since the establishment of IITGN

YOUNG ALUMNI EXCELLENCE AWARDS

The Young Alumni Excellence Award 2024 was formally presented to the awardees at the hands of Prof
Rajat Moona, Director, IITGN, during a special ceremony held on campus on December 5, 2025. The award
recognises the remarkable achievements and contributions of IITGN alumni who have demonstrated
excellence in their respective fields early in their careers. Through this recognition, the institute celebrates
the impact its alumni continue to create across academia, entrepreneurship, and professional domains. IIT
Gandhinagar congratulates the awardees on their outstanding accomplishments and takes pride in their
success, wishing them continued growth and even greater achievements in the years ahead.
The awardees for the Young Alumni Excellence Award 2024 are:
Outstanding Academic Achievement
Sumitava Mukherjee (PhD/CG/2014), Associate Professor, IIT Delhi
Outstanding Entrepreneurship
Ajinkya Kulkarni (BTech/ME/2012), Co-founder & CEO, Wint Wealth
Abhik Patel (BTech/ME/2012), Co-founder & CPO, Wint Wealth
Nisarg Shah (BTech/EE/2014), Co-Founder & CEO, Affable.ai
Outstanding Professional Achievement
Vishnu Kumar Gupta (MTech/ME/2016), Senior Vice President, Testbook
IITGN congratulates the awardees on their exceptional contributions and looks forward to their continued
success in shaping the future.

IIT Gandhinagar

165

ALUMNI EVENTS
ALUMNI MEETUP IN SAN FRANCISCO

The IITGN Alumni Meet took place in the San Francisco Bay Area on January 29, 2026, bringing together over
15 alumni for a warm and engaging evening. The meet was filled with lively conversations, shared memories,
and meaningful reconnections within the IITGN community. Prof Atul Bhargav from the Mechanical
Engineering Department interacted closely with the alumni, adding to the warmth and making the gathering
truly special and memorable.

HOMECOMING 2025

IITGN welcomed over 110 alumni for the seventh edition of Homecoming, held on December 20–21, 2025.
The two-day celebration was filled with nostalgia and camaraderie as alumni reunited with faculty, explored
the new campus, revisited the old VGEC campus, and participated in a special networking dinner. This year’s
Homecoming also featured the Decennial Reunion of the Class of 2015, who were felicitated by Prof Rajat
Moona.
Prof Sudhir Jain, Founding Director of IITGN, attended the event as the Chief Guest and addressed the
alumni during the inaugural session, reflecting on the institute’s founding values and unique learning
culture. The alumni also enjoyed playing gully cricket and volleyball with their classmates. On the morning
of the second day, they had breakfast followed by discipline-wise interactions with their faculty members.
Alumni also met the NYASA team and the NYASA children, and interacted with Team Moksha Motorsport,
offering guidance and encouragement to their juniors in achieving their goals.

POWER OF 5
The Power of 5 is a bold initiative of IITGN to endow 50 scholarships over the coming years with individual
donations of Rs 5 lakh by alumni of IITGN. According to this initiative, alumni donating Rs 5 lakh will create
a perpetual scholarship worth Rs 1 lakh yearly.
●
●
●
●

Alumni donate Rs 5 lakh
This amount is equally matched with a private donor to create Rs 10 lakhs
The Institute matches the combined contribution with Rs 10 lakhs to create a total corpus of Rs 20 lakhs
The corpus of Rs 20 lakhs is suitably invested and creates a perpetual scholarship of Rs 1 lakh annually
